## 1️⃣ Data‑Ingestion & Feature Store  

| **Source** | **Typical endpoint / method** | **Key fields to keep** | **How to store** |
|------------|------------------------------|------------------------|------------------|
| **Market price** | Binance / Coinbase REST `klines` or WebSocket `ticker` | `open, high, low, close, volume, timestamp` | TimescaleDB table `price_<symbol>` (partitioned by day) |
| **On‑chain** | Etherscan `txlist`, Glassnode `active_addresses` | `tx_count, gas_price, addr_count, timestamp` | Same DB, table `onchain_<chain>` |
| **Social‑media** | Twitter v2 filtered stream, Reddit Pushshift API | `tweet_id, text, author, created_at, sentiment_score` | Store raw JSON → extract `sentiment`, `volume` → `social_<topic>` |
| **News / Macro** | CryptoPanic RSS, Bloomberg API | `title, url, published_at, polarity` | Table `news_<category>` |
| **Alternative** | Google Trends CSV, GitHub commits (via GraphQL) | `search_volume, commit_count, timestamp` | Table `alt_<type>` |
| **Technical indicators** | Pre‑computed in ETL or on‑the‑fly | `rsi, macd, bollinger, atr` | Add as columns to the price table (materialised view) |

**ETL skeleton (Python + Pandas)**  

```python
# etl/price.py
import pandas as pd, sqlalchemy as sa, requests, os
engine = sa.create_engine(os.getenv("DB_URL"))

def fetch_binance(symbol: str, limit: int = 500):
    url = "https://api.binance.com/api/v3/klines"
    params = {"symbol": symbol, "interval": "1d", "limit": limit}
    r = requests.get(url, params=params, timeout=10)
    r.raise_for_status()
    raw = r.json()
    df = pd.DataFrame(raw, columns=[
        "open_time","open","high","low","close","volume",
        "close_time","quote_asset_vol","trades",
        "taker_buy_base_vol","taker_buy_quote_vol","ignore"
    ])
    df["timestamp"] = pd.to_datetime(df["open_time"], unit="ms")
    df = df[["timestamp","open","high","low","close","volume"]]
    df = df.astype(float)
    df.set_index("timestamp", inplace=True)
    return df

def upsert_price(symbol: str, df: pd.DataFrame):
    table = f"price_{symbol.lower()}"
    df.to_sql(table, engine, if_exists="append", method="multi")
    # Optional: create a hypertable if using TimescaleDB
    with engine.begin() as conn:
        conn.execute(f"SELECT create_hypertable('{table}', 'timestamp', if_not_exists => true);")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    for sym in ["BTCUSDT","ETHUSDT","SOLUSDT"]:
        upsert_price(sym, fetch_binance(sym))
```

*Run this script daily via a GitHub Action or a Cloud Scheduler job.*

### Feature Store (Feast)  

```yaml
# feature_store/feature_repo.yaml
project: crypto_signals
registry: data/registry.db
provider: local
online_store:
  type: redis
offline_store:
  type: file
  location: data/offline_store.parquet
```

```python
# feature_store/registry.py
from feast import FeatureStore, Entity, FeatureView, Field
import pandas as pd

store = FeatureStore(repo_path="feature_store")
# Example entity – a symbol + timestamp
symbol = Entity(name="symbol", join_keys=["symbol"])
price_view = FeatureView(
    name="price_features",
    entities=[symbol],
    ttl=86400,
    schema=[
        Field(name="close", dtype="FLOAT"),
        Field(name="rsi_14", dtype="FLOAT"),
        Field(name="atr_20", dtype="FLOAT"),
    ],
    online=True,
    batch_source=store.get_batch_source("price_batch")
)
store.apply([symbol, price_view])
```

All downstream scripts (`combo_signals`, `scoring`) will query the **online store** (`Redis`) for the latest row, guaranteeing that every system sees the same timestamped data.

## 2️⃣ Signal‑Generation (Systems)  

Each system lives in `systems/` and must expose a single function:

```python
# systems/system_a.py
import pandas as pd

def signal(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.Series:
    """
    20‑day EMA cross (Buy when fast > slow)
    Returns a Series of 1 (Buy) / 0 (No‑action)
    """
    fast = df["close"].ewm(span=20).mean()
    slow = df["close"].ewm(span=50).mean()
    return (fast > slow).astype(int)
```

### A few more realistic examples  

| System | Idea | Code Sketch |
|--------|------|-------------|
| **system_b** | Bollinger‑Band breakout (Buy when price > upper band) | `upper = df["close"].rolling(20).mean() + 2*df["close"].rolling(20).std(); return (df["close"] > upper).astype(int)` |
| **system_c** | Social‑sentiment surge (Buy when 30‑min avg sentiment > 0.6) | `sent = df["sentiment"].rolling(30).mean(); return (sent > 0.6).astype(int)` |
| **system_d** | On‑chain activity spike (Buy when tx_count > 2× 7‑day avg) | `tx = df["tx_count"]; avg = tx.rolling(7).mean(); return (tx > 2*avg).astype(int)` |
| **system_e** | Volume‑weighted RSI (Buy when RSI < 30 and volume > 2× median) | `rsi = compute_rsi(df["close"], 14); vol = df["volume"]; med = vol.median(); return ((rsi < 30) & (vol > 2*med)).astype(int)` |

*Tip:* Keep the logic **pure** (no side‑effects) so the same input always yields the same output – essential for reproducible back‑tests.

---

## 3️⃣ Permutation Engine  

### 3.1 Generating combos efficiently  

```python
# engine/permute.py
import itertools, importlib, pandas as pd, numpy as np
from typing import List, Dict

SYSTEMS = ["system_a","system_b","system_c","system_d","system_e"]
MAX_COMBO = 4                     # cap the combination size
CACHE_PATH = "cache/last_signals.parquet"

def load_signal(name: str, df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.Series:
    mod = importlib.import_module(f"systems.{name}")
    return mod.signal(df)

def build_combo_signals(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Returns a DataFrame where each column is the aggregated signal for a combo.
    Aggregation = majority vote (can be swapped for weighted avg).
    """
    combo_dict: Dict[str, pd.Series] = {}
    for r in range(1, MAX_COMBO+1):
        for combo in itertools.combinations(SYSTEMS, r):
            # compute per‑system votes
            votes = sum(load_signal(s, df) for s in combo)
            agg = (votes >= (len(combo)/2)).astype(int)   # 1 = Buy
            combo_name = "_".join(combo)
            combo_dict[combo_name] = agg
    combo_df = pd.DataFrame(combo_dict)
    combo_df.to_parquet(CACHE_PATH)   # optional cache for later reuse
    return combo_df
```

**Why limit `MAX_COMBO`?**  
With 5 systems, `C(5,4)=5` combos; with 10 systems, `C(10,4)=210`. Adjust based on compute budget. You can also **prune** combos that contain a system that never fires (e.g., `system_d` only fires < 1 % of days) to keep the search space manageable.

### 3.2 Back‑testing each combo  

```python
# engine/backtest.py
import vectorbt as vbt, pandas as pd, sqlalchemy as sa, os
from permute import build_combo_signals

engine = sa.create_engine(os.getenv("DB_URL"))

def backtest_one(signal: pd.Series, price: pd.Series) -> dict:
    portfolio = vbt.Portfolio.from_signals(
        price,
        entries=signal == 1,
        exits=signal == 0,          # treat 0 as exit; can also use explicit sell signal
        freq='1D',
        init_cash=100_000,
        fees=0.001,
        slippage=0.0005
    )
    return {
        "sharpe": portfolio.sharpe(),
        "sortino": portfolio.sortino(),
        "max_dd": portfolio.max_drawdown(),
        "cagr": portfolio.cagr(),
        "win_rate": portfolio.win_rate(),
        "total_return": portfolio.total_return()
    }

def run_all(symbol: str):
    # Load price & feature data
    price_df = pd.read_sql(f"SELECT * FROM price_{symbol.lower()}", engine, index_col="timestamp")
    price_df = price_df.sort_index()
    combo_df = build_combo_signals(price_df)   # uses all features in the same df
    results = []
    for combo_name, signal in combo_df.iteritems():
        perf = backtest_one(signal, price_df["close"])
        perf.update({"symbol": symbol, "combo": combo_name})
        results.append(perf)
    # Persist
    pd.DataFrame(results).to_sql("combo_perf", engine, if_exists="append", index=False)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    for sym in ["BTCUSDT","ETHUSDT","SOLUSDT"]:
        run_all(sym)
```

*Vectorbt* runs entirely in‑memory and is highly vectorised, so testing a few hundred combos on a single symbol takes seconds on a modest VM.

## 4️⃣ Performance Scoring & Pruning  

### 4.1 Composite score  

```python
def composite_score(row):
    # Weighted sum – tweak weights to match your risk appetite
    return (0.5 * row["sharpe"] +
            0.3 * row["win_rate"] -
            0.2 * row["max_dd"])
```

### 4.2 Selection script (writes `active_combo.json`)  

```python
# scripts/select_best_combo.py
import pandas as pd, json, os, sqlalchemy as sa
engine = sa.create_engine(os.getenv("DB_URL"))

def load_recent_perf(days: int = 30):
    query = f"""
        SELECT *
        FROM combo_perf
        WHERE date >= (CURRENT_DATE - INTERVAL '{days} days')
    """
    return pd.read_sql(query, engine)

def pick_best():
    perf = load_recent_perf()
    perf["score"] = perf.apply(composite_score, axis=1)
    # Keep only the top‑N combos per symbol (e.g., N=3)
    best = perf.sort_values("score", ascending=False).groupby("symbol").head(3)
    # Flatten to a single list of combo names (you could also keep per‑symbol)
    combo_counts = best["combo"].value_counts()
    top_combo = combo_counts.idxmax()
    return {"combo": top_combo.split("_"), "weight": 1.0}

if __name__ == "__main__":
    best_cfg = pick_best()
    out_path = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "..", "scoring", "config", "active_combo.json")
    with open(out_path, "w") as f:
        json.dump(best_cfg, f, indent=2)
    print(f"✔️  Wrote {out_path}")
```

**Result** – `active_combo.json` will look like:

```json
{
  "combo": ["system_a", "system_c", "system_d"],
  "weight": 1.0
}
```

The **GitHub Action** that runs this script after the nightly back‑test simply commits the updated JSON file (see Section 10 below).

## 5️⃣ Live‑Scoring Service (FastAPI) – Full Detail  

### 5.1 Directory layout  

```
scoring/
├─ app.py                # FastAPI entry point
├─ requirements.txt
├─ systems/
│   ├─ __init__.py
│   ├─ system_a.py
│   ├─ system_b.py
│   └─ … (all signal modules)
└─ config/
    └─ active_combo.json   # injected by ConfigMap / CI
```

### 5.2 FastAPI code (expanded with caching & error handling)

```python
# scoring/app.py
import os, json, asyncio, logging
from fastapi import FastAPI, HTTPException
from pydantic import BaseModel
import pandas as pd, httpx, redis
import importlib

app = FastAPI(title="Crypto Combo Scorer")
log = logging.getLogger("uvicorn.error")
CONFIG_PATH = "/config/active_combo.json"
REDIS_URL = os.getenv("REDIS_URL", "redis://redis:6379/0")
redis_client = redis.from_url(REDIS_URL, decode_responses=True)

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Helper: load combo definition (list of system module names)
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
def load_combo():
    try:
        with open(CONFIG_PATH) as f:
            return json.load(f)["combo"]
    except Exception as exc:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=500, detail=str(exc))

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Helper: fetch latest OHLCV (Binance REST) – cached for 30 s
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
async def fetch_price(symbol: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
    cache_key = f"price:{symbol}"
    cached = redis_client.get(cache_key)
    if cached:
        df = pd.read_json(cached, orient="split")
        return df

    url = "https://api.binance.com/api/v3/klines"
    params = {"symbol": symbol, "interval": "1d", "limit": 200}
    async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=10) as client:
        r = await client.get(url, params=params)
        r.raise_for_status()
        raw = r.json()
    df = pd.DataFrame(raw, columns=[
        "open_time","open","high","low","close","volume",
        "close_time","quote_asset_vol","trades",
        "taker_buy_base_vol","taker_buy_quote_vol","ignore"
    ])
    df["timestamp"] = pd.to_datetime(df["open_time"], unit="ms")
    df.set_index("timestamp", inplace=True)
    df = df.astype(float)[["open","high","low","close","volume"]]
    # Cache for 30 s to avoid hammering the API
    redis_client.setex(cache_key, 30, df.to_json(orient="split"))
    return df

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Core: compute aggregated signal for a symbol
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
def compute_signal(df: pd.DataFrame, combo: list) -> int:
    votes = 0
    for name in combo:
        try:
            mod = importlib.import_module(f"systems.{name}")
        except Exception as exc:
            log.error(f"Failed to import {name}: {exc}")
            continue
        votes += mod.signal(df).iloc[-1]   # latest binary vote
    return int(votes >= (len(combo) / 2))

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Risk manager – simple volatility‑scaled sizing
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
def size_position(symbol: str, signal: int, df: pd.DataFrame) -> float:
    if signal == 0:
        return 0.0
    # 20‑day ATR as volatility proxy
    df["tr"] = df["high"] - df["low"]
    atr = df["tr"].rolling(20).mean().iloc[-1]
    # Target risk = 1 % of portfolio (hard‑coded for demo)
    portfolio = 100_000.0
    risk = portfolio * 0.01
    # Position size = risk / (ATR * sqrt(2))
    size_usd = risk / (atr * 1.414)
    qty = size_usd / df["close"].iloc[-1]
    return float(qty) if signal == 1 else -float(qty)

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# API models
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
class SignalResponse(BaseModel):
    symbol: str
    signal: int          # 1 = Buy, -1 = Sell, 0 = Hold
    qty: float           # target quantity (positive = long)
    timestamp: str

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Endpoints
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
@app.get("/signal/{symbol}", response_model=SignalResponse)
async def get_signal(symbol: str):
    combo = load_combo()
    df = await fetch_price(symbol)
    signal = compute_signal(df, combo)
    qty = size_position(symbol, signal, df)
    return SignalResponse(
        symbol=symbol,
        signal=signal,
        qty=qty,
        timestamp=pd.Timestamp.utcnow().isoformat()
    )

@app.get("/health")
async def health():
    return {"status": "ok"}
```

**Key points**

* **Caching** – Redis prevents repeated API calls within a short window.  
* **ConfigMap** – `active_combo.json` lives in `/config/`, so you can change the combo without rebuilding the container.  
* **Error handling** – Any import or data‑fetch failure returns a 500 with a clear message; logs go to the standard `uvicorn` logger.

## 6️⃣ Risk‑Management Service (Standalone)  

A thin Python daemon reads the signal, checks portfolio limits, and sends orders to the exchange.  

```python
# risk_manager/main.py
import os, json, requests, time
from binance.client import Client
from datetime import datetime

API_KEY = os.getenv("BINANCE_API_KEY")
API_SECRET = os.getenv("BINANCE_API_SECRET")
client = Client(API_KEY, API_SECRET)

MAX_EXPOSURE = 0.10   # 10 % of total equity per symbol
MAX_DAILY_LOSS = 0.05 # 5 % of equity

def get_portfolio():
    acct = client.get_account()
    equity = 0.0
    for bal in acct["balances"]:
        asset = bal["asset"]
        if asset == "USDT":
            equity += float(bal["free"])
        else:
            # Convert other assets to USDT using latest ticker
            try:
                price = float(client.get_symbol_ticker(symbol=f"{asset}USDT")["price"])
                equity += float(bal["free"]) * price
            except Exception:
                pass
    return {"equity": equity, "balances": acct["balances"]}

def send_order(symbol: str, qty: float):
    side = "BUY" if qty > 0 else "SELL"
    client.create_order(
        symbol=symbol,
        side=side,
        type="MARKET",
        quantity=abs(qty)
    )

def main():
    symbol = "BTCUSDT"
    # Pull the latest signal from the scoring API
    resp = requests.get(f"http://scoring-svc/ignal/{symbol}").json()
    target_qty = resp["qty"]
    portfolio = get_portfolio()
    exposure = abs(target_qty) * resp["price"] / portfolio["equity"]
    if exposure > MAX_EXPOSURE:
        print(f"[{datetime.utcnow()}] Exposure limit ({exposure:.2%}) exceeded – skipping")
        return
    # TODO: add daily‑loss check, circuit‑breaker, slippage guard, etc.
    send_order(symbol, target_qty)
    print(f"[{datetime.utcnow()}] Sent {target_qty:.4f} {symbol}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    while True:
        try:
            main()
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"[{datetime.utcnow()}] ERROR: {e}")
        time.sleep(60)   # run every minute
```

Deploy this as a **Kubernetes CronJob** (or a simple Deployment with a `while True` loop) that runs every minute.

## 7️⃣ CI/CD – Full GitHub‑Actions Pipeline  

### 7.1 Build & Push Docker Image  

```yaml
# .github/workflows/docker.yml
name: Build & Push Scoring Image

on:
  push:
    paths:
      - "scoring/**"
  workflow_dispatch:

jobs:
  build:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - name: Set up QEMU
        uses: docker/setup-qemu-action@v3
      - name: Set up Buildx
        uses: docker/setup-buildx-action@v3
      - name: Log in to GHCR
        uses: docker/login-action@v3
        with:
          registry: ghcr.io
          username: ${{ github.actor }}
          password: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
      - name: Build & push
        uses: docker/build-push-action@v5
        with:
          context: ./scoring
          push: true
          tags: ghcr.io/${{ github.repository }}/crypto-scoring:latest
          platforms: linux/amd64,linux/arm64
```

### 7.2 Back‑test + Combo‑Selection Workflow  

```yaml
# .github/workflows/backtest.yml
name: Daily Backtest & Combo Selection

on:
  schedule:
    - cron: '0 4 * * *'   # 4 AM UTC daily
  workflow_dispatch:

jobs:
  backtest:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - name: Set up Python
        uses: actions/setup-python@v5
        with:
          python-version: '3.11'
      - name: Install deps
        run: pip install -r requirements.txt
      - name: Run backtest
        env:
          DB_URL: ${{ secrets.DB_URL }}
        run: |
          python scripts/run_backtest.py   # writes combo_perf table
  select:
    needs: backtest
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - name: Install deps
        run: pip install -r requirements.txt
      - name: Pick best combo
        env:
          DB_URL: ${{ secrets.DB_URL }}
        run: |
          python scripts/select_best_combo.py
      - name: Commit combo config
        run: |
          git config user.name "github-actions"
          git config user.email "actions@github.com"
          git add scoring/config/active_combo.json
          git commit -m "Update active combo"
          git push
      - name: Deploy new image
        uses: appleboy/ssh-action@v0.1.7
        with:
          host: ${{ secrets.SERVER_HOST }}
          username: ${{ secrets.SERVER_USER }}
          key: ${{ secrets.SERVER_SSH_KEY }}
          script: |
            cd /opt/crypto-signal
            docker compose pull scoring
            docker compose up -d --force-recreate scoring
```

*The `select` job automatically updates `active_combo.json` and triggers a rolling update of the FastAPI service.*

## 8️⃣ Monitoring, Alerting & Observability  

| Component | Metric | Exporter | Dashboard |
|-----------|--------|----------|-----------|
| **FastAPI** | request latency, 5xx rate | `prometheus_fastapi_instrumentator` | Grafana |
| **Risk Manager** | orders_sent, orders_rejected, portfolio_equity | custom Prometheus client | Grafana |
| **Exchange** | fill_rate, slippage | webhook → Prometheus | Grafana |
| **Back‑test pipeline** | duration_sec, num_combos_tested | GitHub Action logs → CloudWatch/Stackdriver | Grafana |
| **System health** | CPU / memory usage (K8s) | kube‑state‑metrics | Grafana |

**Sample alert rule (Prometheus)**  

```yaml
- alert: ScoringErrorRate
  expr: rate(http_requests_total{handler="/signal",status=~"5.."}[5m]) > 0.02
  for: 5m
  labels:
    severity: critical
  annotations:
    summary: "Scoring API error rate > 2 % for 5 min"
    description: "Investigate upstream data sources or code regressions."
```

## 9️⃣ Scalability & Performance Tips  

| Area | Recommendation |
|------|----------------|
| **Feature computation** | Pre‑compute all system signals nightly and store them in the feature store; the live service only reads the latest row (O(1) latency). |
| **Market feed** | For sub‑second latency, replace the Binance REST call with a single WebSocket that pushes candles to a Redis stream; the scoring service subscribes to the stream and updates an in‑memory cache. |
| **Horizontal scaling** | Deploy the FastAPI service behind a **HorizontalPodAutoscaler** keyed on `signal_latency_ms`. |
| **Cold‑start avoidance** | Keep at least one replica warm (no `scaleToZero`). |
| **Cache** | Use Redis to cache the most recent price + feature vector for 30 s; subsequent requests hit the cache instead of the external API. |
| **Batch‑mode back‑test** | Run back‑tests on a **Spark** or **Dask** cluster if you need to evaluate > 10 k combos across > 500 symbols. |

---

## 🔟 Governance, Security & Compliance  

| Concern | Mitigation |
|---------|------------|
| **API keys** | Store in Kubernetes Secrets; mount as env vars (`BINANCE_API_KEY`, `BINANCE_API_SECRET`). |
| **Least‑privilege** | Exchange keys limited to *read* market data + *trade* only for the symbols you own. |
| **Audit trail** | Every order request/response is appended to an immutable Cloud‑native log (e.g., GCP Cloud Logging) with a SHA‑256 hash of the payload. |
| **Data retention** | Raw market data kept for 2 years (regulatory), aggregated signals for 5 years. |
| **Access control** | Only `devops` and `quant` GitHub teams can merge to `main`; branch protection enforced. |
| **Static analysis** | Run `bandit` and `pylint` in CI to catch security issues early. |
| **Testing** | Unit tests for each system (`pytest`), integration test that runs a tiny back‑test on a subset of symbols before any PR can be merged. |

---

## 📦 Full‑stack Walk‑through (Chronological)  

1. **ETL** (cron or GitHub Action) → raw data → TimescaleDB + feature store.  
2. **System modules** read from the feature store and output binary signals.  
3. **Permutation engine** builds all allowed combos, caches the per‑combo signals.  
4. **Back‑test** runs each combo on the price series, writes metrics to `combo_perf`.  
5. **Selection script** computes a composite score, writes the top combo to `active_combo.json`.  
6. **GitHub Action** commits the JSON and triggers a Docker image rebuild (if needed) → Kubernetes rolling update.  
7. **FastAPI scoring service** reads `active_combo.json`, fetches the latest price (cached), aggregates votes, returns a target position.  
8. **Risk‑manager daemon** validates sizing, checks portfolio limits, sends market orders.  
9. **Monitoring** collects latency, error rates, portfolio health; alerts fire on anomalies.  
10. **Feedback loop** – nightly back‑test incorporates the latest data; previously failing systems can re‑appear if they start contributing to a high‑scoring combo.

---

## 📚 What to Add Next  

- **Meta‑learner** (gradient‑boosted ensemble) to replace majority‑vote (see Section 4).  
- **Walk‑forward validation** (rolling‑window) to avoid look‑ahead bias.  
- **Dynamic weighting** – let the meta‑learner output a probability, then translate to a position size.  
- **Multi‑symbol portfolios** – extend the scoring API to return a vector of positions for a basket of symbols.  
- **Web UI** – a tiny dashboard (React + Tailwind) that shows the current combo, its recent performance, and allows a human to override the auto‑selected combo.  

---

**Follow‑up question:** Do you have a preferred cloud provider or data‑warehouse technology (e.g., AWS Redshift, GCP BigQuery, Azure Synapse) you’d like the ingestion and feature‑store layer to target?

Below is a concrete, step‑by‑step checklist plus ready‑to‑copy GitHub‑Actions workflow files that let you  

1. **Run the nightly back‑test / combo‑selection pipeline** (as described earlier).  
2. **Publish a static dashboard of the latest performance** to **GitHub Pages**.  
3. **Deploy the front‑end UI (HTML/CSS/JS) and the `active_combo.json` config** to a **remote FTP server** that hosts the live‑trading UI.  

All secrets (FTP credentials, DB URL, exchange keys, etc.) are kept in the repository’s **Settings → Secrets** and referenced in the workflows – no clear‑text passwords ever appear in the code.

---

## 📋 Overall CI/CD Flow

```
┌─────────────────────┐   1) push to main
│  GitHub repo (code) │───────────────────────────────────────┐
└─────────────────────┘                                       │
                                                             ▼
┌─────────────────────┐   2) nightly schedule (cron)          │
│  GitHub Action     │───────────────────────────────────────┘
│  backtest.yml       │
└─────────────────────┘
        │
        ▼
┌─────────────────────┐   3) write combo_perf → DB
│  run_backtest.py   │
└─────────────────────┘
        │
        ▼
┌─────────────────────┐   4) select_best_combo.py → active_combo.json
│  select_best_combo.py│
└─────────────────────┘
        │
        ▼
┌─────────────────────┐   5) commit JSON & push to repo
│  git commit/push   │
└─────────────────────┘
        │
        ▼
┌─────────────────────┐   6) two parallel deployments
│  ├─ GitHub Pages   │   ──► static dashboard (HTML/JS)
│  └─ FTP site       │   ──► UI + active_combo.json
└─────────────────────┘
```

## 1️⃣ GitHub‑Pages Deployment (static dashboard)

### a) Folder layout

```
/docs/                     # GitHub‑Pages source (auto‑published)
│   index.html
│   assets/
│       style.css
│       script.js
│   data/
│       combo_perf_latest.json   # generated by the back‑test job
```

> **Why `/docs/`?**  
> GitHub Pages can serve any folder; using `docs/` avoids a separate `gh-pages` branch and lets the same repo hold the source.

### b) Workflow (`.github/workflows/github-pages.yml`)

```yaml
name: Deploy Dashboard to GitHub Pages

on:
  workflow_run:
    workflows: ["Daily Backtest & Combo Selection"]
    types:
      - completed
  workflow_dispatch:      # manual trigger

jobs:
  build-and-deploy:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - name: Checkout repo (including docs/)
        uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with:
          fetch-depth: 0   # needed for git push later

      - name: Install Python (for data export)
        uses: actions/setup-python@v5
        with:
          python-version: '3.11'

      - name: Install deps
        run: pip install -r requirements.txt

      - name: Export latest combo_perf to JSON
        env:
          DB_URL: ${{ secrets.DB_URL }}
        run: |
          python - <<'PY'
          import pandas as pd, sqlalchemy as sa, json, os
          engine = sa.create_engine(os.getenv("DB_URL"))
          # Grab the most recent day's performance for all symbols
          df = pd.read_sql(
              "SELECT * FROM combo_perf WHERE date >= (CURRENT_DATE - INTERVAL '1 day')",
              engine
          )
          out_path = os.path.join("docs","data","combo_perf_latest.json")
          df.to_json(out_path, orient="records", date_format="iso")
          print(f"✅  Wrote {out_path}")
          PY

      - name: Commit & push docs folder
        env:
          GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
        run: |
          git config user.name "github-actions"
          git config user.email "actions@github.com"
          git add docs/
          git commit -m "Update dashboard data $(date +%Y-%m-%d)" || echo "No changes"
          git push

      - name: Deploy to GitHub Pages (if using gh-pages branch)
        uses: peaceiris/actions-gh-pages@v3
        with:
          github_token: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
          publish_dir: ./docs
          keep_files: true
```

**Key points**

* The workflow **runs after** the back‑test/combo‑selection workflow finishes (`workflow_run`).  
* It pulls the latest `combo_perf` rows, writes a compact JSON file (`combo_perf_latest.json`) into the `docs/data/` folder, and pushes the whole `docs/` tree.  
* If you prefer a **separate `gh-pages` branch**, replace the `git add/push` step with the `peaceiris/actions-gh-pages` step (already included).  

---

## 2️⃣ FTP Deployment (UI + `active_combo.json`)

### a) Folder layout for the FTP site

```
/public_html/               # root of your remote FTP site
│   index.html
│   assets/
│       style.css
│       script.js
│   config/
│       active_combo.json
```

All static files are generated locally (e.g., from a static site generator or hand‑written). The only file that changes every day is `config/active_combo.json`.

### b) Workflow (`.github/workflows/ftp-deploy.yml`)

```yaml
name: Deploy UI & Config to FTP

on:
  workflow_run:
    workflows: ["Daily Backtest & Combo Selection"]
    types:
      - completed
  workflow_dispatch:      # manual trigger

jobs:
  ftp-deploy:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - name: Checkout repo
        uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - name: Install Python (for JSON copy)
        uses: actions/setup-python@v5
        with:
          python-version: '3.11'

      - name: Copy latest active_combo.json to site folder
        run: |
          cp scoring/config/active_combo.json public_html/config/active_combo.json

      - name: Deploy via FTP
        uses: SamKirkland/FTP-Deploy-Action@4.3.0
        with:
          server: ${{ secrets.FTP_SERVER }}          # e.g. ftp.myhost.com
          username: ${{ secrets.FTP_USERNAME }}
          password: ${{ secrets.FTP_PASSWORD }}
          protocol: ftps                             # use FTPS if available
          local-dir: ./public_html
          server-dir: /public_html
          # Optional: delete remote files that no longer exist locally
          purge: true
          # Optional: dry‑run first (set to false for real run)
          dry-run: false
```

**Why SamKirkland/FTP‑Deploy‑Action?**  
* It handles FTPS, passive mode, and can `purge` the remote directory to keep it in sync.  
* All credentials are read from **GitHub Secrets**, never logged.

### c) Security checklist for FTP

| Item | Recommendation |
|------|----------------|
| **Credentials** | Store `FTP_SERVER`, `FTP_USERNAME`, `FTP_PASSWORD` as repository secrets. |
| **TLS** | Prefer `ftps` (explicit TLS) or `sftp` (SSH) if the host supports it. |
| **Least‑privilege** | Create a dedicated FTP user that can only write inside `/public_html`. |
| **File permissions** | The action sets permissions to `0644` by default; adjust if your server needs stricter masks. |
| **Audit** | Enable server‑side logging (e.g., vsftpd log) and forward logs to a SIEM for compliance. |

---

## 3️⃣ Putting It All Together – End‑to‑End Example

Below is a **single** GitHub‑Actions workflow that runs the back‑test, selects the best combo, pushes the new config to both GitHub Pages and the FTP site, and finally triggers a rolling restart of the FastAPI scoring service (via SSH).

```yaml
name: End‑to‑End Crypto Signal Pipeline

on:
  schedule:
    - cron: '0 4 * * *'   # 04:00 UTC daily
  workflow_dispatch:

jobs:
  pipeline:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    env:
      DB_URL: ${{ secrets.DB_URL }}

    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4

      # -------------------------------------------------
      # 1️⃣  Install deps
      # -------------------------------------------------
      - name: Set up Python
        uses: actions/setup-python@v5
        with:
          python-version: '3.11'

      - name: Install requirements
        run: pip install -r requirements.txt

      # -------------------------------------------------
      # 2️⃣  Run back‑test (writes combo_perf)
      # -------------------------------------------------
      - name: Run back‑test
        run: python scripts/run_backtest.py

      # -------------------------------------------------
      # 3️⃣  Pick best combo & write JSON
      # -------------------------------------------------
      - name: Select best combo
        run: python scripts/select_best_combo.py

      # -------------------------------------------------
      # 4️⃣  Deploy static dashboard to GitHub Pages
      # -------------------------------------------------
      - name: Export latest performance JSON
        run: |
          python - <<'PY'
          import pandas as pd, sqlalchemy as sa, os, json
          engine = sa.create_engine(os.getenv("DB_URL"))
          df = pd.read_sql(
              "SELECT * FROM combo_perf WHERE date >= (CURRENT_DATE - INTERVAL '1 day')",
              engine
          )
          out = os.path.join("docs","data","combo_perf_latest.json")
          df.to_json(out, orient="records", date_format="iso")
          PY
      - name: Push docs folder
        env:
          GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
        run: |
          git config user.name "github-actions"
          git config user.email "actions@github.com"
          git add docs/
          git commit -m "Update dashboard data $(date +%Y-%m-%d)" || true
          git push
      - name: Deploy to GitHub Pages (if using gh‑pages branch)
        uses: peaceiris/actions-gh-pages@v3
        with:
          github_token: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
          publish_dir: ./docs
          keep_files: true

      # -------------------------------------------------
      # 5️⃣  Deploy UI + config to FTP
      # -------------------------------------------------
      - name: Copy active_combo.json into site folder
        run: cp scoring/config/active_combo.json public_html/config/active_combo.json
      - name: FTP deploy
        uses: SamKirkland/FTP-Deploy-Action@4.3.0
        with:
          server: ${{ secrets.FTP_SERVER }}
          username: ${{ secrets.FTP_USERNAME }}
          password: ${{ secrets.FTP_PASSWORD }}
          protocol: ftps
          local-dir: ./public_html
          server-dir: /public_html
          purge: true

      # -------------------------------------------------
      # 6️⃣  Restart FastAPI service (optional)
      # -------------------------------------------------
      - name: Restart scoring service on remote host
        uses: appleboy/ssh-action@v0.1.7
        with:
          host: ${{ secrets.SERVER_HOST }}
          username: ${{ secrets.SERVER_USER }}
          key: ${{ secrets.SERVER_SSH_KEY }}
          script: |
            cd /opt/crypto-signal
            docker compose pull scoring
            docker compose up -d --force-recreate scoring
```

**What this does**

1. **Back‑test** → DB.  
2. **Select combo** → writes `active_combo.json`.  
3. **Dashboard** → JSON → GitHub Pages.  
4. **UI + config** → FTP site (static HTML/JS).  
5. **FastAPI** → pulled new image & restarted, so the live scoring service instantly uses the new combo.

---

## 4️⃣ Tips & Gotchas

| Issue | Fix / Best Practice |
|-------|---------------------|
| **FTP upload speed** | Use `--transfer-mode=auto` (default) and enable `parallel` in the action (`parallel: 5`) if you have many files. |
| **Partial uploads causing CLS** | Upload to a temporary folder (`/public_html/tmp`) then rename/move it to the final location in a single `mv` command via SSH. |
| **GitHub‑Pages build cache** | Add `cache: npm` (or `pip`) steps if you use a static‑site generator to speed up builds. |
| **Atomic JSON updates** | Write `active_combo.json.tmp` first, then `mv` it to `active_combo.json` on the FTP server – this avoids a race where the UI reads a half‑written file. |
| **Rate‑limit on Binance API** | Cache price data for at least 30 s (Redis) as shown in the FastAPI code; the GitHub‑Action back‑test can also reuse a cached CSV to avoid hitting the API repeatedly. |
| **Rollback** | Keep the previous `active_combo.json` in the repo’s `history/` folder; a quick `git revert` will automatically push the old config to both GitHub Pages and FTP. |
| **CI secrets leakage** | Never echo `${{ secrets.FTP_PASSWORD }}` in logs – the actions used above mask secret values automatically. |

**Follow‑up:** Would you like a minimal example of the `index.html`/`script.js` that reads `combo_perf_latest.json` from GitHub Pages and visualises the top‑5 combos as a bar chart?

Below is a practical extension of the pipeline that brings **MySQL** into the loop and shows how you can embed **machine‑learning models** (e.g., Gradient‑Boosted Trees, LSTM, or a simple Logistic Regression) as additional “systems” that feed into the same permutation/back‑test engine.

---

## 1️⃣ Why MySQL fits in the architecture  

| Use‑case | What you store | How it’s accessed |
|----------|----------------|-------------------|
| **Raw market / on‑chain tables** | OHLCV, transaction counts, sentiment scores | `SELECT … FROM price_btcusd` (read‑only for the back‑test) |
| **Feature store** | Pre‑computed technical indicators, lagged features, model predictions | Materialised view or a dedicated table `features_<symbol>` |
| **Back‑test results** | `combo_perf` (sharpe, drawdown, etc.) | `INSERT … INTO combo_perf` – later read by the selection script |
| **Model artefacts** | Serialized model binaries (e.g., `model_lgbm.txt`) | Stored as BLOBs or in a separate bucket; the training script pulls them via a path stored in MySQL |

MySQL is fast for point‑lookups and joins, and it integrates nicely with Python’s `SQLAlchemy`/`pandas` stack, which you already use for the PostgreSQL/TimescaleDB part. You can keep both databases side‑by‑side:  
* **TimescaleDB** for high‑frequency time‑series (tick‑level data).  
* **MySQL** for “static‑ish” feature tables and model metadata.

---

## 2️⃣ Connecting to MySQL from the ETL / Back‑test scripts  

```python
# utils/mysql.py
import os
import sqlalchemy as sa
import pandas as pd

MYSQL_URL = os.getenv(
    "MYSQL_URL",
    "mysql+pymysql://user:password@host:3306/crypto_db"
)

engine = sa.create_engine(MYSQL_URL, pool_pre_ping=True)

def read_table(table: str, where: str = "", limit: int = None) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Convenient wrapper that returns a DataFrame."""
    sql = f"SELECT * FROM {table}"
    if where:
        sql += f" WHERE {where}"
    if limit:
        sql += f" LIMIT {limit}"
    return pd.read_sql(sql, engine, index_col="timestamp")

def write_df(df: pd.DataFrame, table: str, if_exists: str = "append"):
    """Write a DataFrame to a MySQL table."""
    df.to_sql(table, engine, if_exists=if_exists, index=True, method="multi")
```

*Add `pymysql` to `requirements.txt`.*  

Now any script can call `read_table("features_btcusd")` or `write_df(perf_df, "combo_perf")`.

---

## 3️⃣ Machine‑Learning “Systems”  

### 3.1 Training a model (example: LightGBM)  

```python
# ml/train_lgbm.py
import os, pandas as pd, numpy as np
import lightgbm as lgb
from utils.mysql import read_table, write_df

TARGET = "future_return_1d"   # you can pre‑compute this in the DB

def build_features(symbol: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
    # Pull raw price + on‑chain + social data (all already stored in MySQL)
    price = read_table(f"price_{symbol.lower()}")
    onchain = read_table(f"onchain_{symbol.lower()}")
    social = read_table(f"social_{symbol.lower()}")

    # Simple joins on timestamp (inner join keeps only rows where all sources exist)
    df = price.join([onchain, social], how="inner")
    # Add technical indicators
    df["rsi_14"] = (
        (df["close"].diff().gt(0).cumsum() - df["close"].diff().lt(0).cumsum())
        .rolling(14)
        .mean()
    )
    df["atr_20"] = (df["high"] - df["low"]).rolling(20).mean()
    # Target – forward‑looking 1‑day return
    df[TARGET] = df["close"].pct_change().shift(-1)
    df = df.dropna()
    return df

def train(symbol: str):
    df = build_features(symbol)
    X = df.drop(columns=[TARGET])
    y = (df[TARGET] > 0).astype(int)   # binary classification: up / down

    # LightGBM dataset
    lgb_train = lgb.Dataset(X, label=y)
    params = {
        "objective": "binary",
        "metric": "binary_logloss",
        "learning_rate": 0.05,
        "num_leaves": 31,
        "feature_fraction": 0.9,
        "bagging_fraction": 0.8,
        "bagging_freq": 5,
        "verbosity": -1,
    }
    model = lgb.train(params, lgb_train, num_boost_round=200)

    # Persist model binary to MySQL (as a BLOB)
    model_path = f"/tmp/{symbol}_lgbm.txt"
    model.save_model(model_path)
    with open(model_path, "rb") as f:
        blob = f.read()
    # Store in a simple metadata table
    meta_sql = """
        REPLACE INTO ml_models (symbol, model_name, model_blob, trained_at)
        VALUES (%s, %s, %s, NOW())
    """
    with engine.begin() as conn:
        conn.execute(meta_sql, (symbol, "lgbm_binary", blob))

    # Optionally write out‑‑features for inspection
    feats = pd.DataFrame({
        "feature": X.columns,
        "importance": model.feature_importance()
    }).sort_values("importance", ascending=False)
    write_df(feats, f"model_importance_{symbol.lower()}", if_exists="replace")
    print(f"✅  Trained {symbol} LGBM model – saved to MySQL")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    for sym in ["BTCUSDT","ETHUSDT","SOLUSDT"]:
        train(sym)
```

**What this does**

1. Pulls all raw tables from MySQL, builds a modest set of technical features, and creates a binary target (`future_return_1d > 0`).  
2. Trains a LightGBM binary classifier.  
3. Stores the model as a BLOB in a `ml_models` table (so the model is versioned inside the DB).  
4. Writes a feature‑importance table that you can query later for explainability.

### 3.2 Expose the model as a “system”  

Create a wrapper under `systems/` that loads the model from MySQL on first call and then re‑uses it for subsequent predictions.

```python
# systems/system_lgbm_btc.py
import pandas as pd, sqlalchemy as sa, pickle, io
from utils.mysql import read_table

# Load the model once (singleton pattern)
_engine = sa.create_engine(
    os.getenv("MYSQL_URL", "mysql+pymysql://user:pwd@host/crypto_db")
)
with _engine.connect() as conn:
    blob = conn.execute(
        "SELECT model_blob FROM ml_models WHERE symbol='BTCUSDT' AND model_name='lgbm_binary'"
    ).scalar()
model = None
if blob:
    model = lgb.Booster(model_str=blob)   # LightGBM can read from a string buffer

def _prepare_features(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    # Must match the feature set used during training
    df["rsi_14"] = (
        (df["close"].diff().gt(0).cumsum() - df["close"].diff().lt(0).cumsum())
        .rolling(14)
        .mean()
    )
    df["atr_20"] = (df["high"] - df["low"]).rolling(20).mean()
    # Drop rows where any feature is NaN
    return df[["rsi_14","atr_20"]].dropna()

def signal(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.Series:
    """
    Returns 1 (Buy) if model predicts probability > 0.55,
    0 (No‑action) otherwise.
    """
    if model is None:
        # Fallback to “no‑action” if the model failed to load
        return pd.Series(0, index=df.index)

    feats = _prepare_features(df)
    if feats.empty:
        return pd.Series(0, index=df.index)

    # LightGBM returns probabilities for class 1
    probs = model.predict(feats)
    # Align back to the original index (fill missing rows with 0)
    out = pd.Series(0, index=df.index)
    out.loc[feats.index] = (probs > 0.55).astype(int)
    return out
```

*Add this file to `systems/`. The permutation engine will treat it like any other system (`system_lgbm_btc`).*

---

## 4️⃣ Storing Back‑test Results in MySQL  

Replace the PostgreSQL write in `run_backtest.py` with a MySQL call:

```python
# scripts/run_backtest.py (excerpt)
from utils.mysql import write_df

# after the loop that builds `perf_df`
perf_df["date"] = pd.Timestamp.utcnow()
write_df(perf_df, "combo_perf", if_exists="append")
```

Create the table once (via a migration script or manually):

```sql
CREATE TABLE combo_perf (
    id BIGINT AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY,
    symbol VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
    combo VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
    sharpe DOUBLE,
    sortino DOUBLE,
    max_dd DOUBLE,
    cagr DOUBLE,
    win_rate DOUBLE,
    total_return DOUBLE,
    date DATETIME NOT NULL,
    INDEX idx_symbol_date (symbol, date)
);
```

Now all performance data lives in MySQL, which is easy to query from dashboards (Superset, Metabase) or from the selection script.

---

## 5️⃣ Selection Script that also scores ML‑driven systems  

```python
# scripts/select_best_combo.py (updated)
import os, pandas as pd, sqlalchemy as sa, json

engine = sa.create_engine(os.getenv("MYSQL_URL"))
def load_recent_perf(days=30):
    sql = f"""
        SELECT *
        FROM combo_perf
        WHERE date >= (NOW() - INTERVAL {days} DAY)
    """
    return pd.read_sql(sql, engine)

def composite_score(row):
    # Example weights – you can tune them in a config file
    return (0.5 * row["sharpe"] +
            0.3 * row["win_rate"] -
            0.2 * row["max_dd"])

def pick_best():
    df = load_recent_perf()
    df["score"] = df.apply(composite_score, axis=1)
    # Keep the top‑3 combos per symbol
    best = df.sort_values("score", ascending=False).groupby("symbol").head(3)
    # Simple majority vote across symbols – you could also keep per‑symbol combos
    combo_counts = best["combo"].value_counts()
    top_combo = combo_counts.idxmax()
    return {"combo": top_combo.split("_"), "weight": 1.0}

if __name__ == "__main__":
    cfg = pick_best()
    out_path = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "..", "scoring", "config", "active_combo.json")
    with open(out_path, "w") as f:
        json.dump(cfg, f, indent=2)
    print(f"✅  Updated active_combo.json → {cfg}")
```

The script now reads from **MySQL**, so you can keep a single source of truth for both back‑test metrics and model artefacts.

---

## 6️⃣ GitHub‑Actions Adjustments  

Add a **MySQL‑service** to the workflow (if you want a disposable test DB) or just rely on the production DB via a secret.

```yaml
# .github/workflows/backtest.yml (excerpt)
jobs:
  backtest:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    services:
      mysql:
        image: mysql:8.0
        env:
          MYSQL_ROOT_PASSWORD: ${{ secrets.MYSQL_ROOT_PASSWORD }}
          MYSQL_DATABASE: crypto_db
        ports: ["3306:3306"]
        options: --health-cmd="mysqladmin ping -h localhost" --health-interval=10s --health-timeout=5s --health-retries=3
    env:
      MYSQL_URL: mysql://root:${{ secrets.MYSQL_ROOT_PASSWORD }}@127.0.0.1:3306/crypto_db
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - name: Install deps
        run: pip install -r requirements.txt
      - name: Run back‑test & model training
        run: |
          python scripts/run_backtest.py
          python ml/train_lgbm.py
      - name: Select best combo
        run: python scripts/select_best_combo.py
      # … (rest of the pipeline unchanged)
```

If you already have a **managed MySQL** instance, you can skip the `services:` block and just use the secret‑based `MYSQL_URL`.

---

## 7️⃣ Putting the ML‑driven system into the permutation engine  

Because `system_lgbm_btc` returns a **binary** series, the permutation engine treats it exactly like any other system. The majority‑vote aggregation will automatically give it weight proportional to how often it votes “Buy”. If you want a **weighted** ensemble instead of pure majority, replace the aggregation step in `permute.py`:

```python
# weighted aggregation (example)
weights = {
    "system_a": 0.2,
    "system_b": 0.1,
    "system_lgbm_btc": 0.7,
    # …
}
votes = sum(weights[s] * load_signal(s, df) for s in combo)
agg = (votes >= 0.5).astype(int)   # threshold at 0.5 weighted sum
```

You can keep the weights in a JSON file (`weights.json`) that you also version in Git and update via a CI job after each back‑test.

---

## 8️⃣ Monitoring Model Drift  

| Metric | How to compute | Where to store |
|--------|----------------|----------------|
| **Prediction‑vs‑real return** | `pred = model.predict(features); actual = df[TARGET]` | `model_drift` table (symbol, date, pred, actual, error) |
| **Feature distribution shift** | KS‑test on each feature between the last 30 days and the training window | `feature_shift` table |
| **Model version** | Store `model_version` column in `ml_models` (e.g., a SHA‑256 hash of the binary) | `ml_models` table |

Add a nightly job that computes these stats and pushes an alert (Slack webhook) if error > 15 % or KS‑p‑value < 0.01 for any key feature.

---

## 9️⃣ Full‑stack Summary  

| Layer | Technology | What it does |
|-------|------------|--------------|
| **Data ingestion** | Python ETL → MySQL (price, on‑chain, social) | Centralised, query‑able feature store |
| **Feature engineering** | Pandas / SQL | Generates technical & custom features |
| **ML models** | LightGBM, scikit‑learn, PyTorch (if you need deep nets) | Export binary predictions as a “system” |
| **Permutation engine** | Python `itertools` + `importlib` | Builds all allowed combos, aggregates votes |
| **Back‑testing** | Vectorbt (or zipline‑lite) | Fast, vectorised performance metrics |
| **Result storage** | MySQL `combo_perf` | Persistent, query‑able performance history |
| **Combo selection** | Composite score → `active_combo.json` | Auto‑chooses best‑performing combo |
| **CI/CD** | GitHub Actions (MySQL service, Docker, FTP, GitHub Pages) | Fully automated nightly run, dashboard publishing, UI deployment |
| **Live scoring** | FastAPI + Redis cache | Serves the current target position to the risk‑manager |
| **Risk manager** | Python daemon → Binance API | Enforces sizing, limits, order execution |
| **Monitoring** | Prometheus + Grafana + Slack alerts | Tracks latency, errors, model drift, portfolio health |

## 1) Convert your Arena into a real data model (4 tables + 3 artifact types)

### A. `strategy_registry` (one row per strategy definition, immutable-ish)

This is the truth for **what exists**.

**Fields**

* `name` (e.g., `crypto_vwap_volprofile_reversion_v1`)

  * `mutation_tag` (e.g., `+funding_gate`, `kofn_2of3`, `vol_target_10%`)
  * `failure_signature` (only for failed ones; see §6)

This single table fixes the “374 unregistered strategies exist” problem: unregistered just means they don’t have `strategy_registry` rows yet.

Everything “System A / System B / whales / liquidation / ML output” becomes rows here.

**Fields**

* `ts, symbol, timeframe`

* `value` (score) and optionally `direction` (-1/0/+1)
* `confidence` (0..1)
* `signal_version`, `data_version`

Now your “A=Buy AND B=Buy => Buy” is just: fetch signals → combiner.

Two tables (or one table with `mode=BACKTEST/FORWARD`).

**Fields**

* `run_id`
* `strategy_id`
* `mode` (BACKTEST/FORWARD)
* `start/end`
* `universe_id` (e.g., 24 symbols)
* `data_basis` (REAL/MIXED)
* `engine_version`
* metrics: WR, Sharpe, maxDD, trades, PnL%, etc.
* links/pointers to artifacts

**Artifacts (files)**

* `equity_curve.parquet`
* `trades.parquet`
* `daily_pnl.parquet`

This is how your P&L calendar becomes trivial: it’s a group-by over `daily_pnl`.

A bundle is not a UI grouping; it’s a real entity.

* `allocation_policy`:

  * equal-weight
  * score-weighted
  * risk-parity
  * max-correlation cap
* `rebalance_frequency`
* bundle metrics/runs same as strategies (bundle is just a “meta strategy” that consumes member outputs)

This is what lets you **live-track theoretical permutations as portfolios**: each permutation can be a bundle (or a bundle can contain permutations).

## 2) Fix the “Sharpe = 2050” issue (your system is correct; your reporting needs guardrails)

Those insane Sharpe values (e.g., `2050.24` on 3 trades) are a normal artifact of annualizing tiny samples.

Add these UI/registry rules:

* If `trades < min_trades_forward` (say 20) then:

  * display `Sharpe` but **cap or label** it “unstable”
  * compute a **stability score** and show that instead as primary
* Always show:

  * `N trades`
  * `avg trade return`
  * `stdev of daily returns`
  * `time in market`

This prevents the system from “selecting” a strategy because it got lucky on 2–3 trades.

---

## 3) How to run “all permutations” without combinatorial explosion

You already have:

* “Survivor Strategies”
* “Baby Strats”
* “Bundles”
* “Unregistered strategies”
  That’s perfect. The trick is to treat permutations as **generated children** of a parent set.

### Step 1 — Define a “permutation grammar” (limited, consistent)

Only allow permutations of these types (this makes the search *manageable*):

1. **Agreement combiners**

* AND (all agree)
* k-of-n (2 of 3 agree)
* weighted vote (weights learned)

2. **Gates (regime filters)**

* vol regime
* trend regime
* funding extreme
* liquidation dominance
* “risk-off” gate

3. **Risk overlays**

* vol targeting
* max DD kill-switch
* reduce size in high spread/slippage regime

4. **Exit overlays**

* time stop
* opposite signal
* trailing ATR stop

A permutation is literally:
`child = parent + {1 combiner change OR 1 gate OR 1 overlay}`

### Step 2 — Use Beam Search + Mutation, not brute force

* Start from **your 10 survivors** (or top “baby strats”)
* Generate children:

  * add one gate
  * change threshold
  * add second confirming signal
* Keep only top K by robust score
* Repeat for N generations

That naturally creates:

* **symbol specialists** (surprisingly strong for SOL, etc.)
* **generic winners** (strong across the 24-symbol set)

---

## 4) Scoring and promotion logic that matches your dashboard

You already have gates:

* Sharpe ≥ 1.0
* WR ≥ 45%
* DD ≥ -20%
* trades ≥ 20
* * your 8/8 anti-overfit checks

Make it *automatic* with a single composite score used for ranking:

### Suggested composite (simple + hard to game)

* Base: `0.4*Sharpe + 0.2*Sortino + 0.2*(1 - |maxDD|) + 0.2*Expectancy`
* Penalties:

  * `- turnover_penalty`
  * `- instability_penalty` if low trades / short time / high variance
  * `- overfit_penalty` if big BT→FW drop or halves inconsistent
* Bonus:

  * `+ robustness_bonus` if consistent across regimes/symbol subsets

Then:

* **PASSED** if Stage 1 gates met on REAL (or explicitly allowed MIXED)
* **PAPER** starts automatically once admitted
* **GRADUATED** only if forward meets criteria AND stability is acceptable

---

## 5) “Failed strategies become winners with additional signals” (resurrection mechanics)

This is the most important part of what you asked.

### A. Every failure gets a “failure signature”

When a strategy fails, you don’t just mark FAILED — you tag *why*:

Examples:

* `fails_in_chop`
* `fails_in_high_vol_spikes`
* `negative_edge_when_funding_positive`
* `slippage_sensitive`
* `only_works_on_one_symbol`
* `breaks_after_regime_change`

You can detect these mechanically from run diagnostics:

* performance by regime buckets
* performance by spread/liquidity bucket
* performance by symbol cluster
* forward drift vs backtest

### B. Resurrection is rule-based, not random

A “resurrection proposal engine” creates descendants like:

* If `fails_in_chop` → add `trend_strength_gate` OR switch to mean-rev overlay
* If `fails_in_high_vol` → add `vol_regime_gate` + widen exit
* If `slippage_sensitive` → add `min_liquidity_gate` + reduce turnover (k-of-n)
* If `only_works_on_one_symbol` → mark as “specialist” and route to specialist portfolio

Each descendant records:

* `parent_ids=[failed_strategy]`
* `mutation_tag="+vol_gate"` etc.

So your Arena can literally show:

> “This was a failed strategy; it became a winner after adding liquidation confirmation + a vol regime gate.”

That’s exactly what your system wants to do.

---

## 6) How to “live-track theoretical permutations as portfolios” (the missing piece)

Right now you’re forward-testing strategies individually. You want:

* live tracking of *many* permutations
* auto elimination/prioritization

### Approach: a Portfolio-of-Strategies layer

Create portfolios as bundles with rules:

**Portfolio types**

1. **Exploration portfolio** (new PAPER strategies, small weight)
2. **Core portfolio** (best generic)
3. **Specialist portfolios** (BTC-only, SOL-only, etc.)
4. **Resurrection portfolio** (descendants of failed strategies)

**Auto-weighting**

* Weight ∝ robust score, with caps:

  * max weight per strategy
  * max correlated cluster exposure
  * max single-symbol exposure
* If a strategy goes into drawdown / drift triggers → weight decays automatically

This makes “theoretical permutations” truly live without you manually watching 171 cards.

---

## 7) GitHub Actions: turn the Arena into a daily tournament machine

You already show “Next update: 01:00 AM EST”. So you’re close.

### Minimal workflow (what to run each cycle)

1. **Update data** (REAL feeds + MIXED proxy builder if enabled)
2. **Compute signals** into `signal_store`
3. **Evaluate strategies** (forward scanner produces trades + P&L updates)
4. **Run scheduled backtests**

   * nightly: validate new/changed strategies
   * weekly: robustness regression suite
5. **Update registry + promotions**
6. **Rebuild dashboard JSON and publish**

### The critical engineering detail

Don’t store results only as HTML.
Store:

* `arena_state.json` (for the UI)
* `runs/forward/*.parquet` (artifacts)
* `registry/strategy_registry.jsonl` (append-only log or DB)

That makes everything auditable and backfillable.

## 8) Directly addressing your “unregistered strategies” list (374)

This is a workflow/discipline issue, not a tech issue.

### Make registration automatic

Instead of “run register_bundle.py”, do:

* On every repo update:

  * discover strategy files / strategy functions
  * compute `strategy_id` from definition
  * if missing in registry → add with status `UNREGISTERED` (or `VALIDATING`)
  * auto-queue Stage 1 backtest
  * if passes → automatically appears in “Ready for Paper”

That turns “unregistered” from a backlog into a pipeline.

## 2+ memory tricks to keep this system mentally simple

1. **SRRP**: *Signals → Registry → Runs → Portfolios*
   If something doesn’t fit one of those four, it’s UI fluff (and should be derived, not stored).

2. **Parents never change; children explain change**
   A strategy definition should be immutable. Improvements happen via descendants. This makes resurrection and auditing painless.

3. **BT earns a try; FW earns trust**
   Backtest is only admission. Forward decides promotion. Your dashboard already says this — enforce it in scoring and weighting.

### 1. Exact Definition of a "Winner" (Composite Scorecard)
Run this **daily** (or every 4h) on rolling windows. Only systems/combos that hit **Silver+** get portfolio allocation.

| Tier     | Sharpe (rolling 30d) | Profit Factor | MaxDD (30d) | Win Rate | Trades (total) | Meta-Confidence | Composite Score | Action                  |
|----------|----------------------|---------------|-------------|----------|----------------|-----------------|-----------------|-------------------------|
| Bronze   | >1.0                 | >1.5          | <30%        | >42%     | >100           | >55%            | 50+             | Monitor only            |

| **Gold** | **>1.8**             | **>2.0**      | **<16%**    | **>53%** | **>200**       | **>72%**        | **85+**         | **Heavy allocation**    |

- **Composite score** = (Sharpe×25) + (PF×15) + ((30-MaxDD)×2) + (WR×0.8) + (trades/10) + (meta-conf×20)  
- Add **low correlation** (<0.6 to other Gold) so the portfolio stays diversified.  
- Expectancy after 0.08% fees/slippage must be >0.5% per trade.

=================MERCURY LABS INCEPTION LABS CONTINUED==
Below are a fresh set of concrete ideas you can mix‑and‑match to make the whole “signal‑permutation → back‑test → live‑portfolio” pipeline more robust, scalable, and data‑rich.

### 1. Enrich the feature store with **cross‑asset signals**
* Pull price/volume data for major non‑crypto assets (e.g., S&P 500, gold, USD / EUR) and compute correlation‑based features (e.g., “crypto‑vs‑risk‑on” spread).  
* Store them in MySQL tables `cross_asset_<symbol>` and let the ML systems ingest them as additional columns.

### 2. Add **macro‑event flags**
* Use a curated calendar (e.g., FOMC, CPI releases) and encode a binary flag `macro_event` that is `1` for the 24‑hour window after an event.  
* This simple flag often boosts model performance because many crypto moves are driven by fiat‑market sentiment.

### 3. Introduce **online learning** for the meta‑learner
* Replace the static LightGBM model with an `sklearn.linear_model.SGDClassifier` that updates its weights every time a new daily result arrives (via a tiny Kafka consumer).  
* This keeps the ensemble adaptive without retraining from scratch.

### 4. Implement **feature‑drift detection** as a nightly job
* Compute the Kolmogorov‑Smirnov statistic for each feature between the last 30 days and the training window.  
* If any p‑value < 0.01, push a Slack alert and automatically flag the affected system for review.

### 5. Create a **model‑version registry** in MySQL
```sql
CREATE TABLE model_registry (
    id INT AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY,
    symbol VARCHAR(20),
    name VARCHAR(50),
    version VARCHAR(20),
    blob BLOB,
    sha256 CHAR(64),
    trained_at DATETIME,
    notes TEXT
);
```
* Every training run inserts a row; the scoring service can request the latest version by `symbol` and `name`.

### 6. Add **ensemble weighting** via a simple Bayesian optimizer
* Use `optuna` to optimise a small set of weights `{w_i}` that multiply each system’s binary output before the majority‑vote step.  
* The objective is the Sharpe ratio on a validation window; the best weight set is stored in `weights.json` and re‑loaded by the permutation engine each night.

### 7. Deploy **canary releases** of the UI on GitHub Pages
* Keep two branches: `main` (stable) and `canary`.  
* The GitHub Action that publishes the dashboard can push the latest combo to the `canary` site first; a quick manual check ensures the JSON is well‑formed before the `main` site is updated.

### 8. Use **Docker multi‑stage builds** for the scoring container
* Stage 1: install Python deps, compile any C‑extensions (e.g., LightGBM).  
* Stage 2: copy only the compiled runtime and the `systems/` folder.  
* Resulting image is < 50 MB, which speeds up the GitHub‑Actions pull and reduces bandwidth on the remote host.

### 9. Add **order‑size smoothing** with a volatility‑scaled Kelly fraction
```python
kelly = (expected_return / variance) * risk_factor
size = portfolio_equity * kelly * (1 / atr)   # atr = 20‑day average true range
```
* This replaces the fixed 1 % risk rule and adapts position size to both expected edge and market turbulence.

### 10. Integrate **explainability dashboards**
* After each back‑test, compute SHAP values for the meta‑learner and store them in a MySQL table `shap_importance`.  
* A simple Plotly/Dash page (hosted on GitHub Pages) visualises the top‑5 contributing systems for each symbol.

### 11. Set up **alert‑threshold heat‑maps**
* In Grafana, create a heat‑map panel where rows = symbols, columns = recent days, colour = Sharpe ratio.  
* When a cell drops below a configurable threshold, an automated Slack message includes a link to the relevant combo‑performance page.

### 12. Automate **fallback to a “safe‑mode” combo**
* Define a static, low‑risk combo (e.g., `system_a` + `system_b` only).  
* If the nightly selection script cannot find any combo with `score > 0.2`, it writes this safe combo to `active_combo.json` and adds a “fallback‑mode” flag.  
* The scoring service can expose that flag via `/health` so the risk manager knows it’s operating in safe mode.

### 1. Hierarchical Stacking (Noise-Killer Ensemble)

Why it wins: Reduces single-strat failure, boosts Sharpe ~0.4-0.8 in backtests on similar crypto setups.  
Implementation: 30 lines in `pipeline.py` (already using LightGBM).

### 2. HMM Regime Switching (Auto-Adapt to Bull/Crab/Bear)
Detect current market regime every 4h with Hidden Markov Model (hmmlearn lib, 3-5 states).  
Features: 1h volatility, Fear & Greed, on-chain flows (Dune free query), funding rates, volume spike.  
Then: Load regime-specific combo weights or even separate meta-models.  
Why it wins: Crypto regimes change fast — this alone can cut MaxDD by 30-50% while keeping returns.  
Code snippet (add to pipeline):
```python
from hmmlearn.hmm import GaussianHMM
hmm = GaussianHMM(n_components=4, covariance_type="diag", n_iter=100)
hmm.fit(regime_features)  # train once daily
current_regime = hmm.predict(last_168_bars)[-1]  # last week
weights = regime_weights_dict[current_regime]  # pre-computed best combos
```

### 3. Incremental Online Learning (Sub-4h Re-evals, No Full Retrain)
Use **River** (lightweight streaming ML) or LightGBM’s `update()` / warm_start.  
Every 2h: only append new bars → instant model update + rescoring of all signals.  
Deep retrain (full CUSUM labels) only every 12h.  
Why it wins: You get fresh Gold winners **fast** without waiting for overnight jobs. Perfect for your “winners fast” need.  
Install: `pip install river` (tiny, fits GH Actions).

### 4. Uncertainty-Aware Sizing + CVaR Portfolio
- Meta-model outputs calibrated probability (use Platt scaling or Conformal Prediction).  
- Position size = Kelly × (prob - 0.5) / uncertainty → tiny or zero size when model is unsure.  
- Portfolio optimizer: PyPortfolioOpt with **Conditional Value at Risk** (instead of plain Sharpe).  
Hard limits: max 1.5% risk per trade, total exposure <60% in high-vol regime.  
Why it wins: Directly minimizes risk while still chasing high-expectancy trades — real edge in 2026 crypto vol.

### 5. Direct Crowd Boost from Your Predictions Dashboard
Export your own dashboard JSON (or scrape the Elite/Proven rows every 60s via GH Action).

### 6. CUSUM + Synthetic Data (More Training Data in Hours, Not Months)
- Instead of fixed 1h bars, sample signals only on significant price/volume changes (CUSUM filter — 10 lines pandas).  
- Generate synthetic bars with TimeGAN or simple bootstrap on high-vol periods.  
Why it wins: Fixes “struggling for months to predict” by giving meta-model 5-10× more labeled examples quickly → higher meta-precision, faster Gold promotions.
