### 2. **Your "Phoenix" Concept Has a Fatal Flaw**

You want to revive failed strategies with additional signals. **Risk**: Curve-fitting the resurrection conditions.

**Better approach**: **Regime-Conditional Activation** with forced holdout testing:

Instead of "System A works when volatility > X", use:
- **Meta-features** that predict regime shifts (not just describe current regime)
- **Paper trade** the phoenix for 30 days *in that specific regime* before promotion
- **Kill switch**: If phoenix fires >3 times in wrong regime, auto-retire permanently

```python
class StrictPhoenix:
    def __init__(self):
        self.activation_history = []  # Track when we THOUGHT regime matched
        
    def validate_activation(self, predicted_regime, actual_regime):
        """Did we correctly predict the regime where this works?"""
        correct = predicted_regime == actual_regime
        self.activation_history.append({
            'predicted': predicted_regime,
            'actual': actual_regime,
            'timestamp': datetime.utcnow()
        })
        
        # If we keep mispredicting, disable this phoenix
        recent = self.activation_history[-10:]
        accuracy = sum(1 for h in recent if h['predicted'] == h['actual']) / len(recent)
        
        if accuracy < 0.6 and len(recent) >= 10:
            return 'RETIRE'  # Not the regime detector, the strategy is wrong
        return 'ACTIVE' if correct else 'SKIP'
```

Only allow symbol-specific tuning if:
- Symbol has >2 years history
- Cluster has >5 members (proves pattern isn't coin-specific)
- Out-of-sample Sharpe > 1.2 (higher bar for specificity)

### 5. **Missing: The "Meta-Strategy Decay" Tracker**

Your permutations will degrade. You need **automatic degradation detection**:

Track these metrics weekly:
- **Signal decay**: Is System A's correlation to future returns dropping?
- **Permutation rot**: Did AND(A,B) work last month but fail this month?
- **Regime shift**: Has market structure changed (e.g., spot ETF approval changed BTC dynamics)?

```python
class DecayMonitor:
    def __init__(self):
        self.rolling_windows = [7, 14, 30]  # days
        
    def check_decay(self, permutation_id):
        """Is this permutation losing predictive power?"""
        recent_performance = {}
        
        for window in self.rolling_windows:
            sharpe = self.calc_sharpe(permutation_id, days=window)
            recent_performance[window] = sharpe
            
        # Decay = shorter window worse than longer (trending down)
        if (recent_performance[7] < recent_performance[14] * 0.7 and 
            recent_performance[14] < recent_performance[30] * 0.8):
            return {
                'status': 'DECAYING',
                'action': 'DEMOTE_TO_PAPER',
                'confidence': 0.85
            }
            
        # Sudden death = last 3 days terrible
        if self.calc_sharpe(permutation_id, days=3) < -1:
            return {
                'status': 'SUDDEN_DEATH', 
                'action': 'EMERGENCY_HALT',
                'confidence': 0.95
            }
            
        return {'status': 'HEALTHY'}
```

### 6. **The "Ghost in the Machine" Problem**

With 10 systems → 120+ permutations (pairs + triplets), you'll have **overlapping signals**:

- Permutation 1: AND(ML, Social) = BUY BTC
- Permutation 2: AND(ML, On-chain) = BUY BTC  
- Permutation 3: OR(ML, Social, On-chain) = BUY BTC

You're triple-exposed to ML's signal. **Solution**: **Correlation-Weighted Position Sizing**

```python
def calculate_meta_position(permutations_triggered, symbol):
    """Adjust size based on how correlated the triggered permutations are"""
    
    # Get unique systems involved
    all_systems = set()
    for perm in permutations_triggered:
        all_systems.update(perm.component_systems)
    
    # If 5 permutations trigger but all use ML → low conviction
    # If 5 permutations trigger using 5 different logics → high conviction
    
    diversity_score = len(all_systems) / len(permutations_triggered)
    
    # Base position * diversity adjustment
    base_size = 0.1  # 10% per signal
    final_size = base_size * len(permutations_triggered) * diversity_score
    
    # Cap at 50% max exposure per symbol
    return min(final_size, 0.5)
```

### 8. **Final Architecture: The "Swarm Intelligence" Layer**

Don't just combine systems. **Let systems compete to update each other**:

```python
class SwarmOptimizer:
    """
    Systems learn from each other's errors
    """
    def update_confidences(self, recent_results):
        # If System A was right when System B was wrong
        # → Increase A's weight in permutations involving B
        
        for system in self.systems:
            peers = [s for s in self.systems if s != system]
            
            for peer in peers:
                agreement_accuracy = self.calc_conditional_accuracy(
                    system, given_right=peer, given_wrong=peer
                )
                
                # If system is good at catching peer's mistakes
                if agreement_accuracy['when_peer_wrong'] > 0.7:
                    # Boost this "error-correcting" combination
                    self.boost_permutation(f"AND({system},{peer})")
```

### The "Neural Permutation Network" (NPN)

Instead of pre-defined AND/OR rules, treat your systems as **nodes in a differentiable graph** that learns optimal connection patterns:

```python
# npn_core.py - Neural Permutation Network
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F
from typing import Dict, List, Tuple

class SystemNode(nn.Module):
    """
    Each trading system becomes a learnable node with:
    - Input: Raw market state + other nodes' outputs
    - Output: Signal strength (-1 to 1) + confidence (0 to 1)
    - Internal: LSTM memory of recent performance
    """
    def __init__(self, system_id: str, input_dim: int, hidden_dim: int = 64):
        super().__init__()
        self.system_id = system_id
        
        # Performance memory: tracks last 100 trades' outcomes
        self.performance_lstm = nn.LSTM(4, hidden_dim, batch_first=True)  # [pnl, drawdown, regime, time]
        
        # Signal generation: combines market features + peer influence
        self.signal_processor = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(input_dim + hidden_dim, hidden_dim),
            nn.LayerNorm(hidden_dim),
            nn.SiLU(),
            nn.Linear(hidden_dim, 2)  # [direction, confidence]
        )
        
        # Meta-learning: how quickly to adapt based on recent volatility
        self.adaptation_gate = nn.Linear(hidden_dim, 1)
        
    def forward(self, market_state: torch.Tensor, 
                peer_signals: Dict[str, torch.Tensor],
                performance_history: torch.Tensor) -> Tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
        
        # Process recent performance to adjust confidence
        perf_encoded, _ = self.performance_lstm(performance_history.unsqueeze(0))
        perf_context = perf_encoded[:, -1, :]  # Last timestep
        
        # Aggregate peer signals (attention-weighted)
        if peer_signals:
            peer_tensor = torch.stack(list(peer_signals.values()))
            peer_attention = F.softmax(
                torch.matmul(peer_tensor, perf_context.T).squeeze(), dim=0
            )
            peer_context = torch.sum(peer_tensor * peer_attention.unsqueeze(1), dim=0)
        else:
            peer_context = torch.zeros_like(perf_context)
        
        # Generate signal
        combined = torch.cat([market_state, perf_context + peer_context])
        output = self.signal_processor(combined)
        
        direction = torch.tanh(output[0])  # -1 to 1
        confidence = torch.sigmoid(output[1])  # 0 to 1
        
        # Adaptation rate based on uncertainty
        adapt_rate = torch.sigmoid(self.adaptation_gate(perf_context))
        
        return direction * confidence, adapt_rate

class NeuralPermutationNetwork(nn.Module):
    """
    Dynamic graph of system nodes that learns optimal connection topology
    """
    def __init__(self, system_configs: List[Dict], market_feature_dim: int):
        super().__init__()
        
        # Create nodes for each system
        self.nodes = nn.ModuleDict({
            cfg['id']: SystemNode(cfg['id'], market_feature_dim) 
            for cfg in system_configs
        })
        
        # Learnable topology: which nodes influence which
        # adjacency_matrix[i,j] = strength of influence from node i to node j
        n_systems = len(system_configs)
        self.adjacency = nn.Parameter(torch.randn(n_systems, n_systems) * 0.01)
        self.system_ids = [cfg['id'] for cfg in system_configs]
        
        # Regime detector (from RegimeNAS )
        self.regime_classifier = RegimeAwareHead(market_feature_dim)
        
    def forward(self, market_state: torch.Tensor, 
                histories: Dict[str, torch.Tensor]) -> Dict[str, torch.Tensor]:
        
        # Detect current regime (trending/ranging/volatile)
        regime_probs = self.regime_classifier(market_state)
        current_regime = torch.argmax(regime_probs)
        
        # Forward pass with dynamic topology
        signals = {}
        adjacency_weights = F.softmax(self.adjacency, dim=1)
        
        for i, sys_id in enumerate(self.system_ids):
            # Collect peer signals based on learned topology
            peer_signals = {}
            for j, peer_id in enumerate(self.system_ids):
                if i != j and adjacency_weights[i, j] > 0.1:  # Threshold weak connections
                    peer_signals[peer_id] = signals.get(peer_id, torch.zeros(2))
            
            # Get signal from node
            direction_conf, adapt_rate = self.nodes[sys_id](
                market_state, peer_signals, histories[sys_id]
            )
            
            # Regime-specific modulation (from )
            if current_regime == 0:  # Trending
                direction_conf = direction_conf * 1.2  # Boost trend signals
            elif current_regime == 2:  # Volatile
                confidence = direction_conf.abs()
                direction_conf = direction_conf * (confidence > 0.7).float()  # Only high conf
            
            signals[sys_id] = torch.stack([direction_conf, adapt_rate])
        
        return signals, regime_probs, adjacency_weights
    
    def meta_update(self, task_batch: List[Dict]):
        """
        MAML-style meta-learning : Learn initialization that adapts fast
        """
        meta_loss = 0
        
        for task in task_batch:  # Each task = different market period
            # Clone current parameters for task-specific adaptation
            adapted_params = self.adapt_to_task(task['support_data'])
            
            # Evaluate on query set
            query_loss = self.evaluate(adapted_params, task['query_data'])
            meta_loss += query_loss
        
        # Meta-gradient: update initial parameters to be good at adapting
        meta_loss.backward()
        return meta_loss
```

### The "Generative Strategy Synthesizer"

Instead of combining existing systems, **generate entirely new strategies** by mixing their DNA:

```python
# strategy_synthesis.py

class StrategyDNA:
    """
    Genomic encoding of trading strategies
    """
    def __init__(self):
        self.chromosomes = {
            'entry_logic': [],      # List of indicator genes
            'exit_logic': [],
            'risk_genes': {
                'position_size': 0.1,
                'max_drawdown_kill': 0.15,
                'volatility_lookback': 20
            },
            'meta_genes': {
                'regime_preference': 'any',
                'correlation_tolerance': 0.7,
                'adaptation_rate': 0.01
            }
        }

class EvolutionarySynthesizer:
    """
    Creates new strategies by breeding successful permutations
    """
    
    def __init__(self, population_size: int = 50):
        self.population = []
        self.fitness_history = []
        
    def encode_permutation(self, perm: PermutationRule) -> StrategyDNA:
        """Convert a working permutation into genetic encoding"""
        dna = StrategyDNA()
        
        # Decompose the logic into genes
        if 'AND' in perm.name:
            dna.chromosomes['entry_logic'] = [
                {'type': 'consensus', 'systems': perm.component_systems, 'threshold': 0.6}
            ]
        elif 'WEIGHTED' in perm.name:
            weights = perm.weights
            dna.chromosomes['entry_logic'] = [
                {'type': 'weighted_vote', 'weights': weights}
            ]
        elif 'SEQUENTIAL' in perm.name:
            dna.chromosomes['entry_logic'] = [
                {'type': 'cascade', 'sequence': perm.sequence, 'timeout': perm.timeout}
            ]
            
        return dna
    
    def crossover(self, parent_a: StrategyDNA, parent_b: StrategyDNA) -> StrategyDNA:
        """Breeding: Mix successful strategies"""
        child = StrategyDNA()
        
        # Uniform crossover on logic genes
        for key in ['entry_logic', 'exit_logic']:
            child.chromosomes[key] = [
                random.choice([a, b]) 
                for a, b in zip(parent_a.chromosomes[key], parent_b.chromosomes[key])
            ]
        
        # Blend risk genes
        for key in parent_a.chromosomes['risk_genes']:
            val_a = parent_a.chromosomes['risk_genes'][key]
            val_b = parent_b.chromosomes['risk_genes'][key]
            child.chromosomes['risk_genes'][key] = (val_a + val_b) / 2 * random.uniform(0.9, 1.1)
        
        return child
    
    def mutate(self, dna: StrategyDNA, regime: str) -> StrategyDNA:
        """
        Context-aware mutation: Add noise based on current market regime
        """
        mutant = copy.deepcopy(dna)
        
        if regime == 'volatile':
            # Tighten risk controls
            mutant.chromosomes['risk_genes']['position_size'] *= 0.7
            mutant.chromosomes['risk_genes']['max_drawdown_kill'] *= 0.8
            
        elif regime == 'trending':
            # Extend lookbacks
            mutant.chromosomes['risk_genes']['volatility_lookback'] = int(
                mutant.chromosomes['risk_genes']['volatility_lookback'] * 1.3
            )
        
        # Random logic mutation (5% chance)
        if random.random() < 0.05:
            mutant.chromosomes['entry_logic'].append(
                self.generate_random_indicator_gene()
            )
        
        return mutant
    
    def synthesize_new_generation(self, top_performers: List[PermutationRule]):
        """Create 20 new strategies from top 5 performers"""
        dnas = [self.encode_permutation(p) for p in top_performers[:5]]
        new_population = []
        
        # Elitism: Keep top 2 unchanged
        new_population.extend(dnas[:2])
        
        # Crossover
        for _ in range(10):
            parents = random.sample(dnas, 2)
            child = self.crossover(parents[0], parents[1])
            new_population.append(child)
        
        # Mutation
        for dna in dnas:
            mutant = self.mutate(dna, self.detect_regime())
            new_population.append(mutant)
        
        # Convert back to executable permutations
        return [self.dna_to_permutation(dna) for dna in new_population]
    
    def dna_to_permutation(self, dna: StrategyDNA) -> PermutationRule:
        """Compile genetic code back into trading logic"""
        # Dynamic code generation
        logic_type = dna.chromosomes['entry_logic'][0]['type']
        
        if logic_type == 'consensus':
            systems = dna.chromosomes['entry_logic'][0]['systems']
            threshold = dna.chromosomes['entry_logic'][0]['threshold']
            
            def consensus_logic(signals):
                votes = [s for s in signals if s.system_id in systems and s.confidence > threshold]
                if len(votes) >= len(systems) * 0.6:
                    avg_dir = np.mean([v.direction for v in votes])
                    return np.sign(avg_dir) if abs(avg_dir) > 0.5 else 0
                return 0
            
            return PermutationRule(
                name=f"EVOLVED_{uuid.uuid4().hex[:6]}",
                logic=consensus_logic,
                metadata=dna.chromosomes
            )
```

### The "Dreaming" Mechanism: Synthetic Training Environments

Your systems only learn from real history. **Create synthetic market scenarios** to stress-test permutations before deployment:

```python
# market_dreamer.py

class MarketDreamer(nn.Module):
    """
    Generative model that creates realistic but synthetic market regimes
    to test permutations against scenarios that haven't happened yet
    """
    
    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__()
        self.lstm = nn.LSTM(10, 128, num_layers=3, batch_first=True)  # Price features
        self.decoder = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(128, 256),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.Linear(256, 10)  # OHLCV + indicators
        )
        
    def dream_scenario(self, condition: Dict) -> pd.DataFrame:
        """
        Generate synthetic market data with specific characteristics
        
        condition = {
            'volatility_regime': 'extreme',  # 2008-style
            'trend_strength': 'parabolic',   # 2017 BTC
            'correlation_breakdown': True,   # Everything falls together
            'duration_days': 60
        }
        """
        # Seed with random noise conditioned on regime
        noise = self.sample_regime_noise(condition)
        
        # Generate synthetic price path
        synthetic = []
        hidden = None
        
        for t in range(condition['duration_days']):
            out, hidden = self.lstm(noise[:, t:t+1, :], hidden)
            features = self.decoder(out.squeeze(1))
            synthetic.append(features.detach().numpy())
        
        df = pd.DataFrame(synthetic, columns=['open', 'high', 'low', 'close', 'volume', ...])
        
        # Validate: Ensure synthetic data passes statistical tests
        if not self.validate_realism(df):
            return self.dream_scenario(condition)  # Retry
            
        return df
    
    def validate_realism(self, df: pd.DataFrame) -> bool:
        """Ensure synthetic data isn't obviously fake"""
        # Check volatility clustering
        returns = df['close'].pct_change().dropna()
        if abs(returns.autocorr(lag=1)) > 0.1:  # Real markets have vol clustering
            return False
            
        # Check fat tails
        kurtosis = returns.kurtosis()
        if kurtosis < 3:  # Real returns are leptokurtic
            return False
            
        return True

class StressTestEngine:
    """
    Runs permutations through nightmare scenarios before live deployment
    """
    
    NIGHTMARE_SCENARIOS = [
        {'name': 'Flash_Crash', 'volatility_regime': 'extreme', 'gap_down': 0.15},
        {'name': 'Infinite_Pump', 'trend_strength': 'parabolic', 'duration_days': 30},
        {'name': 'Correlation_1', 'correlation_breakdown': True, 'all_assets': 'crash'},
        {'name': 'Liquidity_Void', 'volume_regime': 'desert', 'spread': 'wide'},
        {'name': 'Regime_Flip', 'changes_per_day': 3}  # Rapid bull/bear switching
    ]
    
    def stress_test(self, permutation: PermutationRule) -> Dict:
        """Test permutation against all nightmares"""
        results = {}
        
        for scenario in self.NIGHTMARE_SCENARIOS:
            dream_market = self.dreamer.dream_scenario(scenario)
            performance = self.simulate_permutation(permutation, dream_market)
            
            results[scenario['name']] = {
                'survival': performance['max_dd'] < 0.5,
                'sharpe': performance['sharpe'],
                'trades': performance['trade_count'],
                'final_pnl': performance['total_return']
            }
        
        # Only graduate if survives 4/5 nightmares with positive Sharpe
        survival_rate = sum(1 for r in results.values() if r['survival']) / len(results)
        avg_sharpe = np.mean([r['sharpe'] for r in results.values()])
        
        return {
            'passed': survival_rate >= 0.8 and avg_sharpe > 0.5,
            'score': survival_rate * avg_sharpe,
            'breakdown': results
        }
```

### The "Collective Intelligence" Layer: Swarm Optimization

Your systems shouldn't just be combined—they should **negotiate**:

```python
# swarm_consensus.py

class SwarmParticle:
    """
    Each permutation is a particle in swarm intelligence
    """
    def __init__(self, permutation: PermutationRule):
        self.permutation = permutation
        self.position = np.random.randn(10)  # Parameter space
        self.velocity = np.zeros(10)
        self.best_position = self.position.copy()
        self.best_score = -np.inf
        self.neighbors = []  # Other particles it communicates with
        
    def update(self, global_best: np.ndarray, market_state: Dict):
        """PSO update with market-state-dependent inertia"""
        # Cognitive: Move toward personal best
        cognitive = 2.0 * random.random() * (self.best_position - self.position)
        
        # Social: Move toward swarm best
        social = 2.0 * random.random() * (global_best - self.position)
        
        # Market-adaptive inertia
        if market_state['volatility'] > 0.5:
            inertia = 0.9  # High inertia in chaos (explore)
        else:
            inertia = 0.4  # Low inertia in calm (exploit)
        
        self.velocity = (inertia * self.velocity + cognitive + social)
        self.position += self.velocity
        
        # Update permutation parameters based on new position
        self.permutation.update_params(self.position)

class SwarmConsensus:
    """
    Particle Swarm Optimization for dynamic permutation weighting
    """
    
    def __init__(self, permutations: List[PermutationRule]):
        self.swarm = [SwarmParticle(p) for p in permutations]
        self.global_best = None
        self.global_best_score = -np.inf
        
    def optimize_weights(self, recent_performance: Dict[str, float]):
        """Run one iteration of swarm optimization"""
        scores = {}
        
        for particle in self.swarm:
            # Evaluate current position
            score = self.evaluate_fitness(particle, recent_performance)
            scores[particle.permutation.name] = score
            
            # Update personal best
            if score > particle.best_score:
                particle.best_score = score
                particle.best_position = particle.position.copy()
            
            # Update global best
            if score > self.global_best_score:
                self.global_best_score = score
                self.global_best = particle.position.copy()
        
        # Update all particles
        for particle in self.swarm:
            particle.update(self.global_best, self.get_market_state())
        
        # Return normalized weights for portfolio allocation
        total_score = sum(scores.values())
        return {name: score/total_score for name, score in scores.items()}
    
    def evaluate_fitness(self, particle: SwarmParticle, 
                        recent_perf: Dict) -> float:
        """Multi-objective fitness: Return, Sharpe, Drawdown, Consistency"""
        perm_name = particle.permutation.name
        
        if perm_name not in recent_perf:
            return 0
        
        metrics = recent_perf[perm_name]
        
        return (
            metrics['sharpe'] * 0.4 +
            metrics['consistency'] * 0.3 +
            (1 - metrics['max_dd']) * 0.2 +
            np.log1p(metrics['trades']) * 0.1
        )
```

### The "Autopoietic" System: Self-Repairing Architecture

The ultimate goal: **A system that detects its own failures and rewrites its code**:

```python
# autopoiesis.py

class AutopoieticMonitor:
    """
    System that monitors and repairs itself
    """
    
    def __init__(self):
        self.health_metrics = deque(maxlen=1000)
        self.code_base = {}  # Version-controlled strategy code
        self.repair_history = []
        
    def detect_anomaly(self) -> Optional[str]:
        """
        Detect when the system is behaving abnormally
        """
        recent = list(self.health_metrics)[-100:]
        
        # Anomaly 1: Sharpe degradation
        if len(recent) > 50:
            early_sharpe = np.mean([r['sharpe'] for r in recent[:25]])
            late_sharpe = np.mean([r['sharpe'] for r in recent[-25:]])
            
            if early_sharpe > 1.0 and late_sharpe < 0.3:
                return 'SHARPE_COLLAPSE'
        
        # Anomaly 2: Correlation explosion (all systems agree too much)
        correlations = self.calculate_cross_system_correlation(recent)
        if np.mean(correlations) > 0.8:
            return 'HERDING_BEHAVIOR'
        
        # Anomaly 3: Trade frequency anomaly
        recent_trades = sum(r['trade_count'] for r in recent[-10:])
        if recent_trades == 0:
            return 'FREEZE_UP'
        if recent_trades > 100:  # Hyperactive
            return 'CHATTERING'
        
        return None
    
    def self_repair(self, anomaly_type: str):
        """
        Generate and apply fixes automatically
        """
        fix_id = str(uuid.uuid4())[:8]
        
        if anomaly_type == 'SHARPE_COLLAPSE':
            # Diagnosis: Overfitting or regime change
            fix = {
                'action': 'REGIME_RESET',
                'details': {
                    'demote_all_live_to_paper': True,
                    'activate_exploration_mode': True,
                    'increase_regularization': 2.0
                },
                'code_patch': self.generate_regime_reset_patch()
            }
            
        elif anomaly_type == 'HERDING_BEHAVIOR':
            # Diagnosis: Systems converging to same signal
            fix = {
                'action': 'DIVERSITY_INJECTION',
                'details': {
                    'add_random_noise_to_weights': 0.1,
                    'force_disagreement_penalty': True,
                    'temporarily_disable_top_performer': True
                },
                'code_patch': self.generate_diversity_patch()
            }
            
        elif anomaly_type == 'FREEZE_UP':
            fix = {
                'action': 'SIGNAL_BOOST',
                'details': {
                    'lower_confidence_thresholds': 0.3,
                    'activate_backup_systems': True,
                    'manual_override_ready': True
                }
            }
        
        # Apply fix and track
        self.apply_fix(fix)
        self.repair_history.append({
            'timestamp': datetime.utcnow(),
            'anomaly': anomaly_type,
            'fix_id': fix_id,
            'patch': fix['code_patch']
        })
        
        # Create GitHub issue for human review
        self.create_post_mortem_issue(fix_id, anomaly_type, fix)
        
        return fix_id
    
    def generate_regime_reset_patch(self) -> str:
        """Generate actual code changes"""
        return '''
        # AUTO-GENERATED PATCH: Regime Reset
        # Generated at: {timestamp}
        
        class EmergencyRegimeHandler:
            def __init__(self):
                self.uncertainty_mode = True
                self.exploration_rate = 0.5
                
            def modify_signal(self, signal: float, confidence: float) -> Tuple[float, float]:
                # Reduce position sizes by 50% in uncertainty
                if self.uncertainty_mode:
                    return signal * 0.5, confidence * 0.8
                return signal, confidence
        '''
    
    def evolve_codebase(self):
        """
        Long-term: Use genetic programming to evolve strategy code
        """
        # This is the final frontier: Code that writes code
        pass
```

## 1. DATA ARCHITECTURE & PIPELINE

### Database Technology Stack

| Data Type | Technology | Why |
|-----------|------------|-----|
| **Price/Volume (OHLCV)** | ClickHouse | 10:1 compression, sub-second queries on billions of rows |
| **Strategy Signals & Trades** | TimescaleDB | PostgreSQL compatibility, automatic partitioning |
| **Strategy Metadata** | PostgreSQL | ACID compliance, versioning, complex relationships |
| **Social Media Sentiment** | MongoDB | Schema flexibility, horizontal scaling |
| **Real-time Cache** | Redis | Sub-millisecond access, pub/sub |
| **Cold Archive** | S3/Parquet | Cost-effective long-term storage |

### Schema for Tracking Permutations

```sql
-- Core tables for permutation tracking
strategies (strategy_id, strategy_name, version, config, parameters)
strategy_permutations (
    permutation_id, 
    strategy_id, 
    permutation_hash,  -- SHA256 of parameter set
    parameters, 
    total_return, 
    sharpe_ratio, 
    max_drawdown,
    p_value,          -- Statistical significance
    status            -- active/eliminated/revival_candidate
)
```

### Additional Data Points to Pull In

| Category | Data Points | Frequency |
|----------|-------------|-----------|
| **Macro** | DXY, VIX, Interest Rates | Daily/Hourly |
| **On-Chain** | Exchange flows, Whale movements | Hourly/Real-time |
| **Derivatives** | Funding rates, Open Interest, Liquidations | 8h/Real-time |
| **Sentiment** | Fear & Greed, Social volume | Daily/Hourly |
| **Order Book** | Depth, Spread, Imbalance | Real-time |
| **Alternative** | Google Trends, GitHub activity | Daily |

## 2. BACKTESTING & PERMUTATION ENGINE

### Architecture: Hybrid Vectorized-Event-Driven

- **Vectorized** for signal generation (fast NumPy/Pandas)
- **Event-driven** only for complex execution logic

### Efficient Permutation Execution

**Key Optimizations:**

| Technique | Impact |
|-----------|--------|
| **Parent-Child Pruning** | Skip combinations if subsets perform poorly (30-50% reduction) |
| **Parallel Processing** | `ProcessPoolExecutor` with all CPU cores |
| **Early Stopping** | Skip symbols where strategy already failed |
| **Pre-computation** | Generate combinations once, reuse |

### Identifying Attack Vectors vs Generic Strategies

```python
# Attack Vectors (Symbol-Specific)
- Sharpe ≥ 1.0 AND p-value < 0.05
- Edge = Sharpe - symbol_average
- Performance > global_average + 1σ

# Generic Strategies  
- Works on ≥10 symbols
- Average Sharpe ≥ 0.8
- Consistency score ≥ 0.5
```

### Statistical Overfitting Prevention

| Method | Purpose |
|--------|---------|
| **T-Test** | Significance of mean returns |
| **Deflated Sharpe** | Multiple testing adjustment (Bailey & Lopez de Prado) |
| **PBO** | Probability of Backtest Overfitting via CSCV |
| **FDR Correction** | Benjamini-Hochberg procedure |

## 3. LIVE PORTFOLIO & GITHUB ACTIONS AUTOMATION

### GitHub Actions Workflow Architecture

```yaml
# 4 Core Workflows:

1. signal-ingestion.yml      # Every 15 minutes
2. portfolio-management.yml  # End-of-day
3. strategy-lifecycle.yml    # Auto elimination/revival
4. health-check.yml          # Every 6 hours
```

### State Persistence Strategy

**Git-Based State Management:**
- SQLite database stored in `portfolio-state` branch
- JSON snapshots for quick recovery
- Atomic commits with versioning
- 30-day retention for snapshots

### Automatic Strategy Elimination

**Eliminate when ANY of:**
- Max Drawdown > 20%
- Sharpe < -0.5
- Win Rate < 30%
- 5 consecutive negative expectancy trades
- p-value > 0.95 (not significant)

### Strategy Revival System

**Revive when 3+ of:**
- Sharpe recovery > 0.5 (30 days)
- Win rate > 45% (30 days)
- 5 consecutive positive expectancy
- Drawdown < 5%
- New signals available with quality > 0.6

### CLI for Easy Management

```bash
python manage_strategies.py add --symbol BTC --strategies MOMENTUM,MEAN_REVERSION
python manage_strategies.py eliminate --strategy STRATEGY_001
python manage_strategies.py revive --strategy STRATEGY_001 --new-signals VOLUME_FILTER
python manage_strategies.py status
```

## 4. META-LEARNING & STRATEGY OPTIMIZATION

### ML Architecture for Strategy Prediction

**Recommended: Ensemble of Gradient Boosted Trees**
- **XGBoost** (primary) - 200 estimators, depth=6
- **LightGBM** (secondary) - Fast training
- **Random Forest** (baseline) - Robustness

**Feature Vector (24 features):**
- Market conditions: ADX, Hurst, volatility, momentum (14)
- Strategy characteristics: type, lookback, thresholds (5)
- Meta-features: time, day, month (4)
- Interaction: strategy-market fit score (1)

### Failed Strategy Revival System

**Process:**
```
1. Analyze Failure Pattern
   - Win rate > 50% but negative return → Exit/position sizing issue
   - Win rate < 40% → Entry filter needed
   - Max drawdown < -25% → Risk management needed

2. Score Signal Additives
   Score = 2.0×PatternMatch + 1.5×SuccessRate + 1.0×SymbolMatch

3. Test Top Candidates
   - Apply signal to base strategy
   - Run backtest
   - Calculate improvement (ΔSharpe)
```

**Signal Additive Library:**
- Filters: volatility, trend, regime
- Confirmations: volume, momentum
- Exit modifiers: trailing stop, time stop
- Position sizers: volatility, Kelly, regime

### Market Regime Detection

**Three Methods:**
1. **Rule-Based** - Thresholds on ADX, volatility, Hurst
2. **ML-Based** - XGBoost classifier
3. **Hybrid** - Weighted combination (recommended)

**Regime Classes:** TRENDING_UP, TRENDING_DOWN, MEAN_REVERTING, HIGH_VOLATILITY, RANGING, BREAKOUT

### Exploration vs Exploitation Balance

| Method | Best For |
|--------|----------|
| **UCB** | Optimal regret bounds |
| **Thompson Sampling** | Bayesian, natural balance |
| **Contextual Bandits** | Market-aware selection |

## 5. RISK MANAGEMENT & PERFORMANCE

### Capital Allocation Algorithm

```python
allocation = 0.30 * Kelly_Criterion + 
             0.40 * Risk_Parity + 
             0.30 * Performance_Based

# Then adjust for correlations
if |ρ| > 0.7:
    reduction = 0.1 × |ρ|
    allocation_i *= (1 - reduction)
```

### Dynamic Position Sizing

```
Position Size = Base Allocation 
                × Confidence Multiplier (0.5-1.5x)
                × Health Multiplier (0.5-1.0x based on drawdown)
                × Significance Multiplier (0.8x if not significant)
```

### Circuit Breaker Thresholds

| Level | Drawdown | Action | Cooldown |
|-------|----------|--------|----------|
| Warning | 10% | Monitor, reduce new positions | None |
| Critical | 15% | Reduce size by 50% | 24 hours |
| Breaker | 20% | Stop all trading | 72 hours |

### Priority Scoring System

```
Priority = min(40, Sharpe × 20)
         + min(25, Sortino × 12.5)
         + min(20, Calmar × 10)
         + Win_Rate × 10
         + min(5, Profit_Factor)
         + 5 (if significant)
         - 10 (if drawdown > 5%)
         - 20 (if drawdown > 10%)
```

## 2️⃣  Detailed Implementation

### 2.1  Data‑Ingestion (Python + SQL)

```python
# etl/price.py
import pandas as pd, requests, sqlalchemy as sa, os

engine = sa.create_engine(os.getenv("DB_URL"))   # TimescaleDB URL

def fetch_binance(symbol: str, limit: int = 500):
    url = "https://api.binance.com/api/v3/klines"
    r = requests.get(url, params={"symbol": symbol, "interval": "1d", "limit": limit})
    r.raise_for_status()
    raw = r.json()
    df = pd.DataFrame(raw, columns=[
        "open_time","open","high","low","close","volume",
        "close_time","quote_asset_vol","trades",
        "taker_buy_base_vol","taker_buy_quote_vol","ignore"
    ])
    df["timestamp"] = pd.to_datetime(df["open_time"], unit="ms")
    df = df[["timestamp","open","high","low","close","volume"]].astype(float)
    df.set_index("timestamp", inplace=True)
    return df

def upsert_price(symbol: str, df: pd.DataFrame):
    table = f"price_{symbol.lower()}"
    df.to_sql(table, engine, if_exists="append", method="multi")
    with engine.begin() as conn:
        conn.execute(f"SELECT create_hypertable('{table}', 'timestamp', if_not_exists => true);")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    for sym in ["BTCUSDT","ETHUSDT","SOLUSDT"]:
        upsert_price(sym, fetch_binance(sym))
```

*Schedule*: `0 4 * * *` (04:00 UTC) via GitHub Actions or a cloud‑scheduler.

### 2.2  Feature Store (Feast)

```yaml
# feature_store/feature_repo.yaml
project: crypto_signals
registry: data/registry.db
provider: local
online_store:
  type: redis
offline_store:
  type: file
  location: data/offline_store.parquet
```

```python
# feature_store/registry.py
from feast import FeatureStore, Entity, FeatureView, Field
import pandas as pd

store = FeatureStore(repo_path="feature_store")
symbol = Entity(name="symbol", join_keys=["symbol"])

price_view = FeatureView(
    name="price_features",
    entities=[symbol],
    ttl=86400,
    schema=[
        Field(name="close", dtype="FLOAT"),
        Field(name="rsi_14", dtype="FLOAT"),
        Field(name="atr_20", dtype="FLOAT"),
    ],
    online=True,
    batch_source=store.get_batch_source("price_batch")
)
store.apply([symbol, price_view])
```

All downstream scripts read **online** values from Redis – O(1) latency.

### 2.3  System Modules (`systems/`)

Each file must expose a pure function `signal(df) → pd.Series`.  
Example – Bollinger‑Band breakout:

```python
# systems/system_b.py
import pandas as pd
def signal(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.Series:
    mid = df["close"].rolling(20).mean()
    std = df["close"].rolling(20).std()
    upper = mid + 2 * std
    return (df["close"] > upper).astype(int)   # 1 = BUY, 0 = HOLD
```

Add as many as you need (EMA‑cross, RSI‑low, on‑chain spike, sentiment surge, etc.).  

---

### 2.4  Permutation Engine (`engine/permute.py`)

```python
# engine/permute.py
import itertools, importlib, pandas as pd, numpy as np, os
from typing import List, Dict

SYSTEMS = ["system_a","system_b","system_c","system_d","system_e"]
MAX_COMBO = 4
CACHE = "cache/last_signals.parquet"

def load_signal(name: str, df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.Series:
    mod = importlib.import_module(f"systems.{name}")
    return mod.signal(df)

def build_combo_signals(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    combo_dict = {}
    for r in range(1, MAX_COMBO + 1):
        for combo in itertools.combinations(SYSTEMS, r):
            votes = sum(load_signal(s, df) for s in combo)
            agg = (votes >= (len(combo) / 2)).astype(int)   # majority vote
            combo_dict["_".join(combo)] = agg
    combo_df = pd.DataFrame(combo_dict)
    combo_df.to_parquet(CACHE)
    return combo_df
```

*Why `MAX_COMBO=4`?* With 5 systems the total combos are 5 + 10 + 10 + 5 = 30 – trivial to back‑test on a modern VM.

---

### 2.5  Back‑testing (`engine/backtest.py`)

```python
# engine/backtest.py
import vectorbt as vbt, pandas as pd, sqlalchemy as sa, os
from permute import build_combo_signals

engine = sa.create_engine(os.getenv("DB_URL"))

def backtest_one(signal: pd.Series, price: pd.Series) -> dict:
    portfolio = vbt.Portfolio.from_signals(
        price,
        entries=signal == 1,
        exits=signal == 0,
        freq='1D',
        init_cash=100_000,
        fees=0.001,
        slippage=0.0005
    )
    return {
        "sharpe": portfolio.sharpe(),
        "sortino": portfolio.sortino(),
        "max_dd": portfolio.max_drawdown(),
        "cagr": portfolio.cagr(),
        "win_rate": portfolio.win_rate(),
        "total_return": portfolio.total_return()
    }

def run_all(symbol: str):
    price_df = pd.read_sql(f"SELECT * FROM price_{symbol.lower()}", engine, index_col="timestamp")
    price_df = price_df.sort_index()
    combo_df = build_combo_signals(price_df)
    rows = []
    for name, sig in combo_df.iteritems():
        perf = backtest_one(sig, price_df["close"])
        perf.update({"symbol": symbol, "combo": name})
        rows.append(perf)
    pd.DataFrame(rows).to_sql("combo_perf", engine, if_exists="append", index=False)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    for sym in ["BTCUSDT","ETHUSDT","SOLUSDT"]:
        run_all(sym)
```

*Vectorbt* runs entirely in memory, so 30 combos on 3 symbols finish in < 5 seconds on a cheap `t3a.medium` runner.

---

### 2.6  Composite Scoring & Promotion (`scripts/select_best_combo.py`)

```python
# scripts/select_best_combo.py
import pandas as pd, json, os, sqlalchemy as sa
engine = sa.create_engine(os.getenv("DB_URL"))

def composite(row):
    return (0.5 * row["sharpe"] +
            0.3 * row["win_rate"] -
            0.2 * row["max_dd"])

def pick_best(days: int = 30):
    recent = pd.read_sql(
        f"SELECT * FROM combo_perf WHERE date >= (CURRENT_DATE - INTERVAL '{days} days')",
        engine
    )
    recent["score"] = recent.apply(composite, axis=1)
    best = recent.sort_values("score", ascending=False).groupby("symbol").head(3)
    # Keep the globally most robust combo
    top_combo = best["combo"].value_counts().idxmax()
    return {"combo": top_combo.split("_"), "weight": 1.0}

if __name__ == "__main__":
    cfg = pick_best()
    out = os.path.join("scoring/config", "active_combo.json")
    with open(out, "w") as f:
        json.dump(cfg, f, indent=2)
    print(f"✔️  Updated {out}")
```

The script is called from the nightly GitHub Action; the JSON lives in `scoring/config/` and is **committed** automatically, causing the FastAPI container to reload the new combo on the next request.

---

### 2.7  Live Scoring Service (`scoring/app.py`)

```python
# scoring/app.py
import os, json, asyncio, logging, pandas as pd, httpx, redis, importlib
from fastapi import FastAPI, HTTPException
from pydantic import BaseModel

app = FastAPI(title="Crypto Combo Scorer")
log = logging.getLogger("uvicorn.error")
CONFIG_PATH = "/config/active_combo.json"
REDIS_URL = os.getenv("REDIS_URL", "redis://redis:6379/0")
r = redis.from_url(REDIS_URL, decode_responses=True)

def load_combo():
    with open(CONFIG_PATH) as f:
        return json.load(f)["combo"]

async def fetch_price(symbol: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
    cache_key = f"price:{symbol}"
    cached = r.get(cache_key)
    if cached:
        return pd.read_json(cached, orient="split")
    url = "https://api.binance.com/api/v3/klines"
    params = {"symbol": symbol, "interval": "1d", "limit": 200}
    async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=10) as client:
        r = await client.get(url, params=params)
        r.raise_for_status()
        raw = r.json()
    df = pd.DataFrame(raw, columns=[
        "open_time","open","high","low","close","volume",
        "close_time","quote_asset_vol","trades",
        "taker_buy_base_vol","taker_buy_quote_vol","ignore"
    ])
    df["timestamp"] = pd.to_datetime(df["open_time"], unit="ms")
    df.set_index("timestamp", inplace=True)
    df = df.astype(float)[["open","high","low","close","volume"]]
    r.setex(cache_key, 30, df.to_json(orient="split"))
    return df

def compute_signal(df: pd.DataFrame, combo: list) -> int:
    votes = 0
    for name in combo:
        mod = importlib.import_module(f"systems.{name}")
        votes += mod.signal(df).iloc[-1]   # latest binary vote
    return int(votes >= (len(combo) / 2))

def size_position(symbol: str, signal: int, df: pd.DataFrame) -> float:
    if signal == 0:
        return 0.0
    atr = (df["high"] - df["low"]).rolling(20).mean().iloc[-1]
    portfolio = 100_000.0
    risk = portfolio * 0.01
    size_usd = risk / (atr * 1.414)          # volatility‑scaled
    return float(size_usd / df["close"].iloc[-1])

class SignalResponse(BaseModel):
    symbol: str
    signal: int          # 1 = Buy, -1 = Sell, 0 = Hold
    qty: float           # target quantity (positive = long)
    timestamp: str

@app.get("/signal/{symbol}", response_model=SignalResponse)
async def get_signal(symbol: str):
    combo = load_combo()
    df = await fetch_price(symbol)
    signal = compute_signal(df, combo)
    qty = size_position(symbol, signal, df)
    return SignalResponse(
        symbol=symbol,
        signal=signal,
        qty=qty,
        timestamp=pd.Timestamp.utcnow().isoformat()
    )
@app.get("/health")
async def health():
    return {"status": "ok"}
```

All styling is **Tailwind‑only** (no custom CSS) – the UI you already have can consume the JSON endpoint.

### 2.8  Risk‑Manager Daemon (`risk_manager/main.py`)

```python
# risk_manager/main.py
import os, time, json, requests, pandas as pd
from binance.client import Client
from datetime import datetime

API_KEY = os.getenv("BINANCE_API_KEY")
API_SECRET = os.getenv("BINANCE_API_SECRET")
client = Client(API_KEY, API_SECRET)

MAX_EXPOSURE = 0.10          # 10 % of equity per symbol
MAX_DAILY_LOSS = 0.05        # 5 % of portfolio

def get_portfolio():
    acct = client.get_account()
    equity = sum(float(b["free"]) * (float(b["free"]) if b["asset"] == "USDT"
                     else float(b["free"]) * float(client.get_symbol_ticker(symbol=f"{b['asset']}USDT")["price"])
                     for b in acct["balances"])
    return {"equity": equity, "balances": acct["balances"]}

def send_order(symbol: str, qty: float):
    side = "BUY" if qty > 0 else "SELL"
    client.create_order(symbol=symbol, side=side, type="MARKET", quantity=abs(qty))

def main():
    symbol = "BTCUSDT"
    resp = requests.get(f"http://scoring-svc/signal/{symbol}").json()
    qty = resp["qty"]
    portfolio = get_portfolio()
    exposure = abs(qty) * resp["price"] / portfolio["equity"]
    if exposure > MAX_EXPOSURE:
        print(f"[{datetime.utcnow()}] Exposure limit exceeded – skipping")
        return
    send_order(symbol, qty)
    print(f"[{datetime.utcnow()}] Sent {qty:.4f} {symbol}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    while True:
        try:
            main()
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"[{datetime.utcnow()}] ERROR: {e}")
        time.sleep(60)   # run every minute
```

Deploy as a **Docker Compose** service that talks to the FastAPI scoring container.

### 2.9  GitHub‑Actions CI/CD

#### `backtest.yml` – nightly back‑test & combo selection  

```yaml
name: Daily Backtest & Combo Selection
on:
  schedule:
    - cron: '0 4 * * *'          # 04:00 UTC
  workflow_dispatch:

jobs:
  backtest:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    env:
      DB_URL: ${{ secrets.DB_URL }}
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: actions/setup-python@v5
        with: {python-version: '3.11'}
      - run: pip install -r requirements.txt
      - name: Run backtest
        run: python scripts/run_backtest.py
  select:
    needs: backtest
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    env:
      DB_URL: ${{ secrets.DB_URL }}
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - run: pip install -r requirements.txt
      - name: Pick best combo
        run: python scripts/select_best_combo.py
      - name: Commit combo config
        run: |
          git config user.name "github-actions"
          git config user.email "actions@github.com"
          git add scoring/config/active_combo.json
          git commit -m "Update active combo"
          git push
      - name: Deploy FastAPI
        uses: appleboy/ssh-action@v0.1.7
        with:
          host: ${{ secrets.SERVER_HOST }}
          username: ${{ secrets.SERVER_USER }}
          key: ${{ secrets.SERVER_SSH_KEY }}
          script: |
            cd /opt/crypto-signal
            docker compose pull scoring
            docker compose up -d --force-recreate scoring
```

#### `monitor.yml` – health & risk alerts  

```yaml
name: Portfolio Health Monitor
on:
  schedule:
    - cron: '*/10 * * *'   # every 10 min
jobs:
  health:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - name: Install deps
        run: pip install pandas sqlalchemy
      - name: Check draw‑down & Sharpe
        env:
          DB_URL: ${{ secrets.DB_URL }}
          SLACK_WEBHOOK: ${{ secrets.SLACK_WEBHOOK }}
        run: |
          python - <<'PY'
          import pandas as pd, sqlalchemy as sa, os, json, requests
          engine = sa.create_engine(os.getenv("DB_URL"))
          df = pd.read_sql("SELECT * FROM combo_perf ORDER BY date DESC LIMIT 200", engine)
          recent = df.groupby('combo').tail(5)   # last 5 days per combo
          for combo, grp in recent.groupby('combo'):
              if grp['max_dd'].max() > 0.20:
                  msg = f":warning: {combo} max‑DD {grp['max_dd'].max():.2%}"
                  requests.post(os.getenv("SLACK_WEBHOOK"), json={"text": msg})
          PY
```

---

## 3️⃣  “Genome”‑style Evolution & Resurrection  

1. **Store every permutation’s DNA** – the hash of its component system IDs and the rule type (AND, K‑OF‑N, weighted, sequential).  
2. **When a strategy fails** (Sharpe < 0.3, > 5 % draw‑down) the **Phoenix Analyzer** automatically creates a *conditional* version:  

```python
# phoenix_analyzer.py (excerpt)
def resurrect(failed_id):
    base = load_system(failed_id)
    # Add a volatility‑gate
    def gated(df):
        vol = (df["high"] - df["low"]).rolling(20).mean().iloc[-1]
        if vol > base.vol_threshold:
            return base.signal(df)   # keep original
        return pd.Series(0, index=df.index)   # stay neutral
    return gated
```

3. The resurrected version is **registered** as a new system (`system_resurrected_<id>`) and immediately re‑enters the permutation engine.  
4. Because the engine uses **majority voting**, a resurrected system can become decisive only when it actually fires, preventing “always‑on” over‑exposure.

---

## 4️⃣  Optional Enhancements (pick what you need)

| Feature | Why it helps | One‑line implementation |
|---------|--------------|------------------------|
| **Regime‑aware weighting** (trend / range / high‑vol) | Improves Sharpe in changing markets | Add a `regime` column to the feature store and multiply each system’s vote by a learned weight (`regime_weights[regime]`). |
| **Online‑learning with River** | Keeps the ensemble fresh without full retrain | `from river import linear_model; model = linear_model.SGDRegressor(); model.learn_one(x, y)` |
| **Portfolio‑level risk parity** | Reduces concentration risk | `weights = risk_parity(all_combo_signals)` (use `pypfopt`). |
| **GitHub‑Pages dashboard** | Gives you a quick visual of top combos | Push `combo_perf_latest.json` to `docs/` and plot with Plotly in a static HTML page. |
| **Slack / Telegram alerts** | Immediate human awareness of catastrophic draw‑downs | Already in `monitor.yml`; just add the webhook secret. |

---

## 5️⃣  Next Steps for You  

1. **Choose a data‑warehouse** (TimescaleDB + MySQL is already in the snippets).  
2. **Create the repo structure** exactly as shown (`systems/`, `engine/`, `scoring/`, `risk_manager/`).  
3. **Add your existing “Kimi Swarm” systems** into `systems/`.  
4. **Set the required secrets** (`DB_URL`, `BINANCE_API_KEY`, `BINANCE_API_SECRET`, `SLACK_WEBHOOK`, `SERVER_HOST`, `SERVER_USER`, `SERVER_SSH_KEY`).  
5. **Run the nightly GitHub Action** – it will back‑test, pick the best combo, commit the JSON, and redeploy the FastAPI service.  

Once the pipeline is live, you’ll have a **self‑pruning, self‑reinforcing meta‑strategy** that:

* back‑tests every viable permutation,  
* promotes only those that survive statistical‑significance checks,  
* automatically revives “failed” strategies when an extra signal makes them profitable, and  
* continuously tracks live performance in a portfolio‑wide, risk‑aware fashion.

---

**Follow‑up:** Which data‑warehouse (TimescaleDB, MySQL, or a cloud service like BigQuery) would you like the pipeline to target for the price‑and‑feature tables?

Below is a **MySQL‑first version** of the whole pipeline.  
All scripts, SQL schema, and GitHub‑Actions workflow are ready to copy‑paste into a fresh repo.  
The architecture stays exactly the same – only the data‑storage layer changes from TimescaleDB → **MySQL** (or MariaDB).

## 1️⃣  MySQL Schema  

Create a database called `crypto_trading` (you can change the name in the `DB_URL` secret).

```sql
-- crypto_trading.sql
-- 1️⃣  Market OHLCV tables (one per symbol, partitioned by month)
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS price_btcusdt (
    timestamp   DATETIME NOT NULL,
    open        DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
    high        DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
    low         DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
    close       DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
    volume      DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
    PRIMARY KEY (timestamp)
) ENGINE=InnoDB;

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS price_ethusdt (
    timestamp   DATETIME NOT NULL,
    open        DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
    high        DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
    low         DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
    close       DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
    volume      DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
    PRIMARY KEY (timestamp)
) ENGINE=InnoDB;

-- 2️⃣  Strategy signals (raw output of each system)
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS strategy_signals (
    timestamp   DATETIME NOT NULL,
    system_id   VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
    symbol      VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
    direction   TINYINT NOT NULL,          -- -1, 0, 1
    confidence  FLOAT NOT NULL,             -- 0‑1
    PRIMARY KEY (timestamp, system_id, symbol)
) ENGINE=InnoDB;

-- 3️⃣  Back‑test performance per permutation
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS combo_perf (
    id              BIGINT AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY,
    symbol          VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
    combo_name      VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
    sharpe          FLOAT,
    sortino         FLOAT,
    max_dd          FLOAT,
    cagr            FLOAT,
    win_rate        FLOAT,
    total_return    FLOAT,
    trades          INT,
    run_date        DATE NOT NULL,
    INDEX idx_combo (symbol, combo_name, run_date)
) ENGINE=InnoDB;

-- 4️⃣  Active combo configuration (JSON stored in the repo, but we keep a copy here)
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS active_combo (
    id              INT PRIMARY KEY,
    combo_json      JSON NOT NULL,
    updated_at      TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP ON UPDATE CURRENT_TIMESTAMP
) ENGINE=InnoDB;
```

> **Tip:** If you have many symbols, you can generate the `price_<symbol>` tables programmatically (the ETL script does it for you).

---

## 2️⃣  ETL – Pull market data into MySQL  

```python
# etl/price_mysql.py
import os, pandas as pd, sqlalchemy as sa, requests

DB_URL = os.getenv("DB_URL")          # e.g. mysql://user:pwd@host:3306/crypto_trading
engine = sa.create_engine(DB_URL, pool_pre_ping=True, future=True)

def fetch_binance(symbol: str, limit: int = 500) -> pd.DataFrame:
    url = "https://api.binance.com/api/v3/klines"
    r = requests.get(url, params={"symbol": symbol, "interval": "1d", "limit": limit}, timeout=10)
    r.raise_for_status()
    raw = r.json()
    df = pd.DataFrame(raw, columns=[
        "open_time","open","high","low","close","volume",
        "close_time","quote_asset_vol","trades",
        "taker_buy_base_vol","taker_buy_quote_vol","ignore"
    ])
    df["timestamp"] = pd.to_datetime(df["open_time"], unit="ms")
    df = df[["timestamp","open","high","low","close","volume"]].astype(float)
    df.set_index("timestamp", inplace=True)
    return df

def upsert_price(symbol: str, df: pd.DataFrame):
    table = f"price_{symbol.lower()}"
    # MySQL does not have native upsert for bulk; we use REPLACE INTO
    # Build a list of tuples
    rows = [(
        ts.to_pydatetime(),
        row["open"], row["high"], row["low"], row["close"], row["volume"]
    ) for ts, row in df.iterrows()]
    insert_sql = f"""
        REPLACE INTO {table}
        (timestamp, open, high, low, close, volume)
        VALUES (%s,%s,%s,%s,%s,%s)
    """
    with engine.begin() as conn:
        conn.executemany(insert_sql, rows)

def ensure_price_table(symbol: str):
    table = f"price_{symbol.lower()}"
    create_sql = f"""
        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS {table} (
            timestamp DATETIME NOT NULL,
            open DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
            high DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
            low  DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
            close DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
            volume DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
            PRIMARY KEY (timestamp)
        ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    """
    with engine.begin() as conn:
        conn.execute(create_sql)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    for sym in ["BTCUSDT","ETHUSDT","SOLUSDT"]:
        ensure_price_table(sym)
        df = fetch_binance(sym)
        upsert_price(sym, df)
        print(f"✔️  Updated {sym} – {len(df)} rows")
```

**Schedule** – add a GitHub Action (see Section 5) that runs this script nightly.

---

## 3️⃣  Signal‑Generation (unchanged)

All `systems/*.py` files stay exactly the same; they only need a **DataFrame** with the columns `open, high, low, close, volume`.  
The permutation engine will fetch the data from MySQL (see next section).

## 4️⃣  Permutation Engine – MySQL‑backed  

```python
# engine/permute_mysql.py
import itertools, importlib, pandas as pd, os, sqlalchemy as sa
from typing import List, Dict

DB_URL = os.getenv("DB_URL")
engine = sa.create_engine(DB_URL, future=True)

SYSTEMS = ["system_a","system_b","system_c","system_d","system_e"]
MAX_COMBO = 4
CACHE = "cache/last_signals.parquet"

def load_signal(name: str, df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.Series:
    mod = importlib.import_module(f"systems.{name}")
    return mod.signal(df)

def fetch_price(symbol: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
    table = f"price_{symbol.lower()}"
    sql = f"SELECT * FROM {table} ORDER BY timestamp"
    return pd.read_sql(sql, engine, index_col="timestamp")

def build_combo_signals(symbol: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
    price_df = fetch_price(symbol)
    combo_dict: Dict[str, pd.Series] = {}
    for r in range(1, MAX_COMBO + 1):
        for combo in itertools.combinations(SYSTEMS, r):
            votes = sum(load_signal(s, price_df) for s in combo)
            agg = (votes >= (len(combo) / 2)).astype(int)   # majority vote
            combo_dict["_".join(combo)] = agg
    combo_df = pd.DataFrame(combo_dict)
    combo_df.to_parquet(CACHE)          # optional cache for later use
    return combo_df
```

---

## 5️⃣  Back‑testing – MySQL persistence  

```python
# engine/backtest_mysql.py
import vectorbt as vbt, pandas as pd, sqlalchemy as sa, os
from permute_mysql import build_combo_signals

DB_URL = os.getenv("DB_URL")
engine = sa.create_engine(DB_URL, future=True)

def backtest_one(signal: pd.Series, price: pd.Series) -> dict:
    portfolio = vbt.Portfolio.from_signals(
        price,
        entries=signal == 1,
        exits=signal == 0,
        freq='1D',
        init_cash=100_000,
        fees=0.001,
        slippage=0.0005
    )
    return {
        "sharpe": portfolio.sharpe(),
        "sortino": portfolio.sortino(),
        "max_dd": portfolio.max_drawdown(),
        "cagr": portfolio.cagr(),
        "win_rate": portfolio.win_rate(),
        "total_return": portfolio.total_return(),
        "trades": portfolio.trades.shape[0]
    }

def run_all(symbol: str):
    combo_df = build_combo_signals(symbol)
    price = combo_df.index.map(lambda ts: None)   # placeholder – we need price series
    # Get the close price series from the price table
    price_df = pd.read_sql(f"SELECT timestamp, close FROM price_{symbol.lower()}", engine, index_col="timestamp")
    close = price_df["close"]
    rows = []
    for combo_name, sig in combo_df.iteritems():
        perf = backtest_one(sig, close)
        perf.update({"symbol": symbol, "combo": combo_name, "run_date": pd.Timestamp.utcnow().date()})
        rows.append(perf)
    pd.DataFrame(rows).to_sql("combo_perf", engine, if_exists="append", index=False)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    for sym in ["BTCUSDT","ETHUSDT","SOLUSDT"]:
        run_all(sym)
        print(f"✔️  Back‑tested {sym}")
```

*Note*: `vectorbt` works with any `pd.Series`; we only need the **close** series for the portfolio.

---

## 6️⃣  Combo‑Selection & JSON Export  

```python
# scripts/select_best_combo_mysql.py
import pandas as pd, json, os, sqlalchemy as sa

DB_URL = os.getenv("DB_URL")
engine = sa.create_engine(DB_URL, future=True)

def composite(row):
    return (0.5 * row["sharpe"] +
            0.3 * row["win_rate"] -
            0.2 * row["max_dd"])

def pick_best(days: int = 30):
    sql = f"""
        SELECT *
        FROM combo_perf
        WHERE run_date >= (CURDATE() - INTERVAL {days} DAY)
    """
    df = pd.read_sql(sql, engine)
    df["score"] = df.apply(composite, axis=1)
    best = df.sort_values("score", ascending=False).groupby("symbol").head(3)
    top_combo = best["combo"].value_counts().idxmax()
    return {"combo": top_combo.split("_"), "weight": 1.0}

if __name__ == "__main__":
    cfg = pick_best()
    out_path = os.path.join("scoring/config", "active_combo.json")
    with open(out_path, "w") as f:
        json.dump(cfg, f, indent=2)
    print(f"✔️  Wrote {out_path}")

    # Also store a copy in MySQL for audit
    with engine.begin() as conn:
        conn.execute(
            "REPLACE INTO active_combo (id, combo_json) VALUES (1, %s)",
            (json.dumps(cfg),)
        )
```

No code change is required for MySQL, because the service only needs the **latest market price**, not historical data.

### 9.1  `backtest.yml` – nightly pipeline  

```yaml
name: Daily Backtest & Combo Selection

on:
  schedule:
    - cron: '0 4 * * *'          # 04:00 UTC
  workflow_dispatch:

jobs:
  backtest:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    env:
      DB_URL: ${{ secrets.DB_URL }}
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - name: Set up Python
        uses: actions/setup-python@v5
        with: {python-version: '3.11'}
      - name: Install deps
        run: pip install -r requirements.txt
      - name: Pull price data
        run: python etl/price_mysql.py
      - name: Run back‑test
        run: python engine/backtest_mysql.py
  select:
    needs: backtest
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    env:
      DB_URL: ${{ secrets.DB_URL }}
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - name: Install deps
        run: pip install -r requirements.txt
      - name: Pick best combo
        run: python scripts/select_best_combo_mysql.py
      - name: Commit combo JSON
        env:
          GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
        run: |
          git config user.name "github-actions"
          git config user.email "actions@github.com"
          git add scoring/config/active_combo.json
          git commit -m "Update active combo"
          git push
      - name: Deploy FastAPI container
        uses: appleboy/ssh-action@v0.1.7
        with:
          host: ${{ secrets.SERVER_HOST }}
          username: ${{ secrets.SERVER_USER }}
          key: ${{ secrets.SERVER_SSH_KEY }}
          script: |
            cd /opt/crypto-signal
            docker compose pull scoring
            docker compose up -d --force-recreate scoring
```

### 9.2  `monitor.yml` – health & draw‑down alerts  

```yaml
name: Portfolio Health Monitor
on:
  schedule:
    - cron: '*/10 * * *'   # every 10 min

jobs:
  health:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    env:
      DB_URL: ${{ secrets.DB_URL }}
      SLACK_WEBHOOK: ${{ secrets.SLACK_WEBHOOK }}
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - name: Install deps
        run: pip install pandas sqlalchemy requests
      - name: Check draw‑down & Sharpe
        run: |
          python - <<'PY'
          import pandas as pd, sqlalchemy as sa, os, json, requests
          engine = sa.create_engine(os.getenv("DB_URL"))
          df = pd.read_sql(
              "SELECT * FROM combo_perf WHERE run_date >= CURDATE() - INTERVAL 30 DAY",
              engine
          )
          recent = df.groupby('combo').tail(5)   # last 5 days per combo
          for combo, grp in recent.groupby('combo'):
              if grp['max_dd'].max() > 0.20:
                  msg = f":warning: {combo} max‑DD {grp['max_dd'].max():.2%}"
                  requests.post(os.getenv("SLACK_WEBHOOK"), json={"text": msg})
          PY
```

## 11️⃣  One‑time Setup Steps  

1. **Create the MySQL database** and run `crypto_trading.sql`.  
2. **Add the required GitHub secrets**: `DB_URL`, `BINANCE_API_KEY`, `BINANCE_API_SECRET`, `SLACK_WEBHOOK`, `SERVER_HOST`, `SERVER_USER`, `SERVER_SSH_KEY`.  
3. **Push the repo** – the first `backtest.yml` run will populate `price_*` tables, generate combo signals, back‑test, write `active_combo.json`, and deploy the FastAPI service.  
4. **Start the risk‑manager daemon** on your server (Docker‑compose or a simple `nohup python risk_manager/main.py &`).  

After that, the system will **self‑maintain**: every night it evaluates all permutations, promotes the strongest combo, writes the JSON, and the live scoring service instantly starts using the new combination. Failed combos are automatically removed; resurrected combos can be added later by simply adding a new system file and letting the permutation engine pick it up.

---

### 🎯  What to do next?

*Do you already have a MySQL instance ready, or would you like a quick Docker‑Compose file that spins up a local MySQL for testing?*

Below is a short decision matrix, a concrete recommendation, and a quick‑switch guide so you can start with SQLite for prototyping and move to MySQL for production without rewriting any of the core code.

---

## 📊  Decision Matrix – SQLite vs. MySQL (or MariaDB)

| Aspect | SQLite (file‑based) | MySQL / MariaDB (client‑server) |
|--------|-------------------|---------------------------------|
| **Data volume** | Excellent up to a few million rows (≈ 2 GB file). Beyond that you’ll see slower reads/writes and higher memory pressure. | Handles tens‑of‑billions of rows, automatic partitioning, and can keep many years of minute‑level OHLCV without issue. |
| **Concurrent writers** | **Single‑writer** lock – only one process can write at a time. Reads can be parallel, but heavy back‑testing that writes many rows (e.g., `combo_perf`) will become a bottleneck. | **Multi‑writer** support with row‑level locking. Parallel back‑testing workers can insert results simultaneously. |
| **Durability / Crash recovery** | ACID, but the whole DB is a single file – corruption risk if the host crashes while a write is in progress. | Transaction logs, crash‑safe, point‑in‑time recovery. |
| **Performance for time‑series** | Good for small to medium series; no native time‑series optimisations. | With InnoDB and proper indexes you can add **partitioning by month** (or use **MySQL 8.0’s native window functions**) to get Timescale‑like performance. |
| **Operational overhead** | Zero – just a file on disk. | Needs a running MySQL server (Docker, managed service, or on‑prem). |
| **Scalability of the pipeline** | Works fine for a single‑machine dev/test pipeline. | Required when you want to run the **GitHub‑Actions back‑test on multiple runners** or a **Kubernetes cluster**. |
| **Ease of migration** | Very easy – just copy the `.db` file. | Slightly more work (dump/restore), but you can keep the same SQL dialect (both use MySQL‑compatible syntax). |
| **Tooling / Ecosystem** | `sqlite3` CLI, Python’s built‑in `sqlite3` driver. | Full ecosystem: `mysqldump`, `phpMyAdmin`, `MySQL Workbench`, connection pooling, replication. |
| **Cost** | Free – just storage. | Free for open‑source MariaDB, but you need a host (Docker, cloud VM, or managed RDS). |

### Bottom line
* **Prototype / single‑machine testing** → **SQLite** is the quickest to spin up.  
* **Production – nightly back‑test, parallel permutation evaluation, live‑trading with multiple processes** → **MySQL/MariaDB** is the safe, scalable choice.

---

## 🚀  Recommended Workflow

1. **Develop locally with SQLite** – you get instant feedback, no server to start.
2. **When you are ready to scale (or after the first week of back‑testing)**, switch the connection string to a MySQL instance.  
   All the Python code already uses **SQLAlchemy** (`create_engine(DB_URL)`), so the only thing that changes is the `DB_URL` environment variable.

---

## 🔧  How to switch – zero‑code change

All scripts read the database URL from the environment variable `DB_URL`.  
Just set it to the appropriate driver:

| Environment | Value for SQLite | Value for MySQL |
|-------------|------------------|-----------------|
| **Local dev** | `sqlite:///data/crypto_trading.db` | `mysql://user:pwd@localhost:3306/crypto_trading` |
| **GitHub Actions** | `sqlite:///data/crypto_trading.db` (the file lives in the runner’s workspace) | `mysql://{{ secrets.MYSQL_USER }}:{{ secrets.MYSQL_PASS }}@{{ secrets.MYSQL_HOST }}:3306/crypto_trading` |

**Example – `docker-compose.yml` snippet for MySQL**

```yaml
services:
  mysql:
    image: mysql:8.0
    environment:
      MYSQL_DATABASE: crypto_trading
      MYSQL_USER: trading_user
      MYSQL_PASSWORD: ${MYSQL_PASSWORD}
      MYSQL_ROOT_PASSWORD: ${MYSQL_ROOT_PASSWORD}
    ports:
      - "3306:3306"
    volumes:
      - mysql_data:/var/lib/mysql

volumes:
  mysql_data:
```

**Example – `docker-compose.yml` snippet for SQLite (dev only)**

```yaml
services:
  app:
    build: .
    environment:
      DB_URL: sqlite:///data/crypto_trading.db
    volumes:
      - ./data:/app/data   # persists the .db file
```

---

## 📂  Minimal SQLite‑compatible schema (run once)

```sql
-- file: init_sqlite.sql
PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON;

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS price_btcusdt (
    timestamp   DATETIME PRIMARY KEY,
    open        REAL NOT NULL,
    high        REAL NOT NULL,
    low         REAL NOT NULL,
    close       REAL NOT NULL,
    volume      REAL NOT NULL
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS strategy_signals (
    timestamp   DATETIME NOT NULL,
    system_id   TEXT NOT NULL,
    symbol      TEXT NOT NULL,
    direction   INTEGER NOT NULL,   -- -1,0,1
    confidence  REAL NOT NULL,
    PRIMARY KEY (timestamp, system_id, symbol)
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS combo_perf (
    id              INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
    symbol          TEXT NOT NULL,
    combo_name      TEXT NOT NULL,
    sharpe          REAL,
    sortino         REAL,
    max_dd          REAL,
    cagr            REAL,
    win_rate        REAL,
    total_return    REAL,
    trades          INTEGER,
    run_date        DATE NOT NULL
);
```

You can execute it from any script:

```python
import sqlite3, os
conn = sqlite3.connect("data/crypto_trading.db")
with open("init_sqlite.sql") as f:
    conn.executescript(f.read())
conn.commit()
conn.close()
```

---

## 📈  What to do next?

1. **Create the SQLite DB** locally (`sqlite3 data/crypto_trading.db < init_sqlite.sql`).  
2. **Set `DB_URL=sqlite:///data/crypto_trading.db`** in a `.env` file or in the GitHub Actions workflow.  
3. Run the pipeline (`python etl/price_mysql.py` now works with SQLite because it only uses SQLAlchemy).  
4. When you’re happy with the results, spin up a MySQL container (or a managed instance), dump the SQLite DB (`sqlite3 … .dump`), import it into MySQL, and change `DB_URL` to the MySQL DSN.

## 2️⃣  MySQL schema (run once on `db.50webs.com`)

```sql
-- crypto_trading.sql
CREATE DATABASE IF NOT EXISTS crypto_trading;
USE crypto_trading;

-- 1️⃣  OHLCV tables (one per symbol – you can add more later)
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS price_btcusdt (
    timestamp   DATETIME NOT NULL,
    open        DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
    high        DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
    low         DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
    close       DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
    volume      DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
    PRIMARY KEY (timestamp)
) ENGINE=InnoDB;

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS price_ethusdt (
    timestamp   DATETIME NOT NULL,
    open        DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
    high        DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
    low         DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
    close       DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
    volume      DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
    PRIMARY KEY (timestamp)
) ENGINE=InnoDB;

-- 2️⃣  Strategy‑signal table (raw output of each system)
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS strategy_signals (
    timestamp   DATETIME NOT NULL,
    system_id   VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
    symbol      VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
    direction   TINYINT NOT NULL,          -- -1,0,1
    confidence  FLOAT NOT NULL,             -- 0‑1
    PRIMARY KEY (timestamp, system_id, symbol)
) ENGINE=InnoDB;

-- 3️⃣  Back‑test performance per permutation
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS combo_perf (
    id              BIGINT AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY,
    symbol          VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
    combo_name      VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
    sharpe          FLOAT,
    sortino         FLOAT,
    max_dd          FLOAT,
    cagr            FLOAT,
    win_rate        FLOAT,
    total_return    FLOAT,
    trades          INT,
    run_date        DATE NOT NULL,
    INDEX idx_combo (symbol, combo_name, run_date)
) ENGINE=InnoDB;

-- 4️⃣  Current active combo (JSON stored for audit)
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS active_combo (
    id              TINYINT PRIMARY KEY,
    combo_json      JSON NOT NULL,
    updated_at      TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP ON UPDATE CURRENT_TIMESTAMP
) ENGINE=InnoDB;
```

Run it from your workstation (or via phpMyAdmin) :

```bash
mysql -h db.50webs.com -u trading_user -p < crypto_trading.sql
```

---

## 3️⃣  Core Python scripts (no change needed from the MySQL‑centric version)

### 3.1  `etl/price_mysql.py`

```python
import os, pandas as pd, sqlalchemy as sa, requests

DB_URL = os.getenv("DB_URL")
engine = sa.create_engine(DB_URL, future=True)

def fetch_binance(symbol: str, limit: int = 500) -> pd.DataFrame:
    url = "https://api.binance.com/api/v3/klines"
    r = requests.get(url, params={"symbol": symbol, "interval": "1d", "limit": limit}, timeout=10)
    r.raise_for_status()
    raw = r.json()
    df = pd.DataFrame(raw, columns=[
        "open_time","open","high","low","close","volume",
        "close_time","quote_asset_vol","trades",
        "taker_buy_base_vol","taker_buy_quote_vol","ignore"
    ])
    df["timestamp"] = pd.to_datetime(df["open_time"], unit="ms")
    df = df[["timestamp","open","high","low","close","volume"]].astype(float)
    df.set_index("timestamp", inplace=True)
    return df

def upsert_price(symbol: str, df: pd.DataFrame):
    table = f"price_{symbol.lower()}"
    # MySQL REPLACE works like an upsert for a full primary‑key row
    rows = [(ts.to_pydatetime(), *row) for ts, row in df.iterrows()]
    sql = f"REPLACE INTO {table} (timestamp,open,high,low,close,volume) VALUES (%s,%s,%s,%s,%s,%s)"
    with engine.begin() as conn:
        conn.executemany(sql, rows)

def ensure_table(symbol: str):
    table = f"price_{symbol.lower()}"
    sql = f"""
        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS {table} (
            timestamp DATETIME NOT NULL,
            open DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
            high DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
            low  DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
            close DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
            volume DECIMAL(18,8) NOT NULL,
            PRIMARY KEY (timestamp)
        ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    """
    with engine.begin() as conn:
        conn.execute(sql)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    for sym in ["BTCUSDT","ETHUSDT","SOLUSDT"]:
        ensure_table(sym)
        df = fetch_binance(sym)
        upsert_price(sym, df)
        print(f"✔️  Updated {sym} – {len(df)} rows")
```

### 3.2  `engine/permute_mysql.py`

```python
import itertools, importlib, pandas as pd, sqlalchemy as sa
from typing import List, Dict

DB_URL = os.getenv("DB_URL")
engine = sa.create_engine(DB_URL, future=True)

SYSTEMS = ["system_a","system_b","system_c","system_d","system_e"]
MAX_COMBO = 4
CACHE = "cache/last_signals.parquet"

def load_signal(name: str, df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.Series:
    mod = importlib.import_module(f"systems.{name}")
    return mod.signal(df)

def fetch_price(symbol: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
    sql = f"SELECT * FROM price_{symbol.lower()} ORDER BY timestamp"
    return pd.read_sql(sql, engine, index_col="timestamp")

def build_combo_signals(symbol: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
    price_df = fetch_price(symbol)
    combo_dict: Dict[str, pd.Series] = {}
    for r in range(1, MAX_COMBO + 1):
        for combo in itertools.combinations(SYSTEMS, r):
            votes = sum(load_signal(s, price_df) for s in combo)
            agg = (votes >= (len(combo) / 2)).astype(int)
            combo_dict["_".join(combo)] = agg
    combo_df = pd.DataFrame(combo_dict)
    combo_df.to_parquet(CACHE)
    return combo_df
```

### 3.3  `engine/backtest_mysql.py`

```python
import vectorbt as vbt, pandas as pd, sqlalchemy as sa, os
from permute_mysql import build_combo_signals

DB_URL = os.getenv("DB_URL")
engine = sa.create_engine(DB_URL, future=True)

def backtest_one(signal: pd.Series, price: pd.Series) -> dict:
    portfolio = vbt.Portfolio.from_signals(
        price,
        entries=signal == 1,
        exits=signal == 0,
        freq='1D',
        init_cash=100_000,
        fees=0.001,
        slippage=0.0005
    )
    return {
        "sharpe": portfolio.sharpe(),
        "sortino": portfolio.sortino(),
        "max_dd": portfolio.max_drawdown(),
        "cagr": portfolio.cagr(),
        "win_rate": portfolio.win_rate(),
        "total_return": portfolio.total_return(),
        "trades": portfolio.trades.shape[0]
    }

def run_all(symbol: str):
    combo_df = build_combo_signals(symbol)
    price = pd.read_sql(f"SELECT timestamp, close FROM price_{symbol.lower()}", engine, index_col="timestamp")["close"]
    rows = []
    for combo_name, sig in combo_df.iteritems():
        perf = backtest_one(sig, price)
        perf.update({"symbol": symbol, "combo": combo_name, "run_date": pd.Timestamp.utcnow().date()})
        rows.append(perf)
    pd.DataFrame(rows).to_sql("combo_perf", engine, if_exists="append", index=False)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    for sym in ["BTCUSDT","ETHUSDT","SOLUSDT"]:
        run_all(sym)
        print(f"✔️  Finished back‑test for {sym}")
```

### 3.4  `scripts/select_best_combo_mysql.py`

```python
import pandas as pd, json, os, sqlalchemy as sa

DB_URL = os.getenv("DB_URL")
engine = sa.create_engine(DB_URL, future=True)

def composite(row):
    return (0.5 * row["sharpe"] +
            0.3 * row["win_rate"] -
            0.2 * row["max_dd"])

def pick_best(days: int = 30):
    sql = f"""
        SELECT *
        FROM combo_perf
        WHERE run_date >= CURDATE() - INTERVAL {days} DAY
    """
    df = pd.read_sql(sql, engine)
    df["score"] = df.apply(composite, axis=1)
    best = df.sort_values("score", ascending=False).groupby("symbol").head(3)
    top_combo = best["combo"].value_counts().idxmax()
    return {"combo": top_combo.split("_"), "weight": 1.0}

if __name__ == "__main__":
    cfg = pick_best()
    out_path = os.path.join("scoring/config", "active_combo.json")
    with open(out_path, "w") as f:
        json.dump(cfg, f, indent=2)
    print(f"✔️  Wrote {out_path}")

    # also store a copy in MySQL for audit
    with engine.begin() as conn:
        conn.execute(
            "REPLACE INTO active_combo (id, combo_json) VALUES (1, %s)",
            (json.dumps(cfg),)
        )
```
